### 2026-08-09 — `7a` closed, `7b` routed (Planner, `queue.advance` consumed)

**Gate `43/43` at `fa5edac` and the tree clean — run here, not adopted from the Finalizer's log**
(`logs/planner-step07b-gate.log`, rc=0; `git status --porcelain` empty before and after). This is
the RELATION `7b`'s acceptance 5 names; the count is recorded as what 43/43 meant at this parent,
never as the literal a later row must match.

**Three rows unblock at once, and the routing order is not arbitrary.** `7b`, `7c` and `7d` all
blocked on `7a` alone and are all ready now. Routing `7b` first, because:

1. **`7b` is the only one of the three that can INVALIDATE the other two's material.** It may add
   `absent()`-shaped companion rules — i.e. it can change how many rules the file carries and what
   each one's `expr` is. `7d` puts `severity`/`summary` on **every** rule and wires a
   `promtool test rules` unit suite; `7c` gates the render on `promtool`. Both are cheaper and
   less rework-prone once the rule set has stopped moving. Running `7d` first would annotate a set
   `7b` then grows, and its unit suite would be written against a file about to change.
2. **Three filed rows terminate in `7b` and nowhere else** — `task-1786159639-39db`,
   `task-1786167833-1152`, `task-1786203094-588c`. They are the three the Step 5 → 7 cut named as
   the arming reason for taking Step 7 before Step 6 at all; leaving them open one more row is
   paying the deferral's cost without collecting its return.
3. **`7c` is a safety row for a hazard `7b` makes MORE likely, not less.** Premise 2 measured that
   one unparseable `expr` **stops** Prometheus under `restart: unless-stopped`. `7b` is the row
   that hand-writes new `expr` bodies, so it is the row most likely to produce the bad render
   `7c` exists to catch — but `7c` guards the *play*, and `7b`'s acceptance 2 drives its rules
   through a real container before any play. The ordering does not leave a live gap; it just
   means `7c` lands over the final rule set.

**What I did NOT do.** No `ensure` — the wave was cut whole at `2e8280c` and all five rows exist;
re-cutting it would be the re-`ensure` class (`ralph-planner-reensure-only-for-false-contracts`),
and nothing in `7b`'s acceptance is a false contract: its harness is a live
`prom/prometheus:v3.12.0`, measured working at the cut and again by the Finalizer at `7a`. No
Step 6 movement. No repair of the guard-strength filings (`task-1786228581-69ff`,
`task-1786226998-1e07`, `task-1786227786-ec79`, `task-1786227786-0641`, `task-1786227778-812a`);

### 2026-08-09 — `7b` closed, `7d` routed (Planner, `queue.advance` consumed)

**`7b` IS CLOSED — `cf34ca3`** (three `absent()` doors plus a guard that reads the file for them;
`39db`/`1152`/`588c` all terminated there). GATE **43/43** rc=0 re-run here at this parent
(`logs/planner-step07cd-gate.log`), tree clean before and after — the RELATION `7d`'s and `7c`'s
acceptance both name, recorded as what 43/43 meant at `cf34ca3`, never as a literal a later row must
match. `7c` and `7d` were both unblocked when `7a` closed; `7b` closing moves no new row into the
ready set. The wave is **not** exhausted (`7c`, `7d`, and the operator Demo `7e` remain), so no step
boundary moves this turn.

**`7d` is routed before `7c`, and the reason is `ralph-planner-order-mutators-before-decorators`.**
`7d` is the row that CHANGES THE SHARED RULES ARTIFACT'S SHAPE — it puts `severity`/`summary` on
every one of the seven rules in the rules template and brings the `promtool test rules` behavioural
suite online in `just test`. `7c` is a pure gate on the *render pipeline* (`validate:` on the rules
render task in `docker_host/tasks/main.yml`, ordered before the restart handler) and touches no rule
content. Routing the content-mutator first means `7c`'s gate is authored and tested over the FINAL,
annotated rule file rather than one about to grow — the same shape of argument the `7a → 7b` routing
made when it held `7c`/`7d` behind the row that was still moving the rule set.

1. **The two rows are near-orthogonal and both low-rework in EITHER order** — `7d` edits the rules
   template + `justfile`/test wiring; `7c` edits `tasks/main.yml` + a shape test. Disjoint files.
   `7d`'s annotations do not change whether the render passes `promtool check`, and `7c`'s validate
   is content-agnostic — so neither invalidates the other's material. The tie is broken by the
   mutators-before-decorators memory, and by the mild gain that `7d`'s `promtool test rules` suite
   is in the gate BEFORE `7c` reorders the render, so any rule-loading regression `7c` could cause
   reds immediately.
2. **There is no in-loop safety gap from routing `7d` first.** Premise 2 measured that a malformed
   rules render STOPS Prometheus, and `7c` is the render-time guard for it — but `7c`'s `validate:`
   only fires at `just play`, no play runs during the loop (it is operator-gated at `7e`), and `7d`
   itself wires `promtool check rules`/`promtool test rules` into `just test`, so a malformed edit
   reds the gate at CI time regardless of order. Same no-live-gap argument the `7a → 7b` routing
   made for holding `7c`.
3. **Nothing in the filed backlog is armed by this routing.** The Step 11 guard-strength rows
   (`1e07`, `ec79`, `0641`, `812a`, `69ff`, `63ba`, `0902`, `747a`) stay priced on the bar
   (`ralph-planner-cut-a-step-for-homeless-rows`); the P1 incident and the four operator Demo gates
   stay open and operator-gated, and the ready set is not entirely operator-gated
   (`7c`/`7d` are repo-side), so the loop advances (`ralph-advance-past-a-deploy-independent-gate`).

### 2026-08-08 — why Step 7 before Step 6

Routing out of `plan.md`'s numeric order is a real deviation, so it is recorded with what was
measured rather than asserted (`DEC-322`, confidence 85; `.ralph/agent/decisions.md`).

**Step 6's own sequencing sentence is unsatisfied.** The step says it "lands after the cheaper
in-container signals have been observed — so if `pve-root` latency proves uninteresting, it reverts
having cost nothing else." Those signals have **not** been observed: the Step 1, Step 4 and Step 5
Demos are all pending an operator deploy, and `task-1786190433-8514` makes even their status
ambiguous. The precondition the plan names for putting Step 6 sixth is measurably not met.

**And the hypervisor is not addressable today — measured, not assumed.**
`ansible/inventory/hosts.yml` carries exactly two hosts and is GENERATED ("Edit the Tofu
config/outputs, not this file") by `scripts/gen_inventory.py` from four outputs —
`docker_host_name`/`_ipv4`, `plex_name`/`_ipv4`. `tofu/outputs.tf` has **no PVE host output**
(`proxmox_version` in `auth_check.tf` is a provider probe, not an endpoint), and `ansible/site.yml`
has two plays, neither targeting a hypervisor. Step 6 therefore needs a new tofu output, a
generator change, a new inventory group and a new play **before** its role does anything — and it
is the first wave that puts the box hosting **both** CTs inside `just play`'s blast radius, while
the incident recording that exact accident is open and undischarged.

**Step 7, by contrast, is armed and cheap.** Three rows already in the ready set are filed against
it (`39db`, `1152`, `588c`), all resting on one claim measured true at this cut. Its near end is
live and just closed — the three `blackbox-plex*` jobs shipped at `e652956`, the three
`plex_watchdog_*` / `plex_sqlite_wal_bytes` families at Step 4 — and Step 5's own `Integration`
line reads "consumed by Steps 7 and 9". Every artefact is repo-side and gate-testable; only the
*Demo* needs a deploy. Deferring Step 6 costs Step 7 nothing: premise 5 in `plan.md` shows none of
§5.3's seven rules cites a Step 6 metric.

**Step 6 is next after Step 7 closes**, and its wave cut should start by measuring whether the
inventory generator or `tofu/outputs.tf` is the right place for the PVE endpoint.

#### THE ROW ORDERED ONE MEASUREMENT AND IT RESOLVES IN THE ROW'S FAVOUR — BUT ITS OBVIOUS REPAIR IS A DATA-STRANDING TRAP

`5d`'s record says to read the image's WORKDIR before writing, because if the TSDB is not inside the
named volume the row is repairing the wrong thing. Measured on the pinned image
(`logs/planner-step05d-premises.log` §1–§3): `WorkingDir` is **`/prometheus`**, `Config.Cmd` is
`[--config.file=…, --storage.tsdb.path=/prometheus]`, `Config.User` is `nobody`, and the binary's own
`--help` gives `--storage.tsdb.path="data/"` — relative.

I did not stop at the inference. With a host directory bind-mounted at `/prometheus`, run both ways:

| Run | argv | where the TSDB landed |
|---|---|---|
| K | no `--storage.tsdb.path` (**today**: the compose `command:` overrides the image CMD) | `/prometheus/data/{wal,chunks_head,queries.active}` |
| L | CONTROL, the image's own CMD `--storage.tsdb.path=/prometheus` | `/prometheus/{wal,chunks_head,queries.active}` |

So the TSDB **is** inside the `prometheus-data` volume, one level down at `/prometheus/data`, and it
survives a recreate. The conditional in acceptance 5 therefore **does not fire**: no
`--storage.tsdb.path` is needed and the row is not repairing the wrong thing.

**The trap is the other direction, and it is exactly the edit a careful Builder would make.**
"Restore the flag the image CMD had" means writing `--storage.tsdb.path=/prometheus`, and run L is
what that does: the server starts on an empty directory while every existing block sits at
`/prometheus/data`, still on the volume and invisible to the process. No error, no crash loop — the
silent-healthy shape this objective keeps charging. **If a path flag is pinned at all it must be
`/prometheus/data`**, and the row's own measurement says it need not be pinned.

The suite already owns this fact in one place — `SCRAPE_ONLY_KEYS`' `no_command` paragraph
(`scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py:5171-5175`) says `command:` "REPLACES the image's CMD". Cite
that sentence rather than writing a second one; two literals that agree is the `c6c4`/`e627` class.

#### THE DELIVERY RELATION IS NO LONGER AN ARGUMENT — IT IS FOUR ROWS OF REAL `docker compose`

The record calls this "the one sentence most likely to get copied wrong" and forbids adding a
handler by analogy. It was reasoned, not driven. Driven now, one service, changing only `command:`:

| Row | action | result |
|---|---|---|
| A | create | id `ea064a88` |
| B | **CONTROL** — `up -d`, file UNCHANGED | **same id**; `up -d` really is a no-op over an unchanged spec |
| C | `command:` changed, `up -d` | compose prints **`Recreated`**, id `4ce73c98` |
| D | `command:` changed, `docker compose restart` | **same id**, and the running process still carries the OLD argv `--storage.tsdb.retention.time=90d` while the file on disk says `30d` |

B is what makes C readable — without it, "Recreated" proves nothing about whether `up -d` recreates
everything every time. **D is the strong row**: a handler added by analogy would not merely be
redundant, it would deliver *nothing* while looking like delivery — a restart cannot change argv.
So: **no handler**, and the reason is row D rather than the prose.

#### THE FLAG `plan.md` ORDERS BY NAME IS DEPRECATED IN THE PINNED IMAGE, AND THE SUCCESSOR WOULD INVERT THAT SENTENCE

This is the find of the turn and it is written up as **premise 7** under `plan.md`'s Step 5, because
it is about the Guidance and not only about the row. In short: `--storage.tsdb.retention.time` prints
`[DEPRECATED] … use the storage.tsdb.retention.time field in the config file instead`; it still works
(`duration=90d`) and logs **no deprecation line at all**; the successor is live in v3.12.0
(`storage: {tsdb: {retention: {time: 90d}}}`, `promtool` rc=0, four refuted spellings as controls);
with both set **the config file wins**; and the control with neither set is `duration=15d`, so the
Objective's "silently defaults to 15 d" is now measured rather than asserted.

`5d` still ships the flag — the successor lives in `prometheus.yml`, a `:ro` mount delivered by
`notify: Restart prometheus`, which is the *opposite* delivery path and puts this step's retention
back inside the file `5c` just closed. What the row owes is to say deprecated-but-live and name the
successor.

#### THE GUARD MACHINERY EXISTS — ACCEPTANCE 4 IS A REUSE, NOT A NEW READER

`_service_command_args()` (`:4139`) already returns a compose service's `command:` entries, list or
inline, **decommented** via `_strip_comments`, unquoted; `_last_flag_value()` (`:4167`) already takes
the LAST `--flag=value`. Acceptance 4 — "reads the retention out of the rendered command list, not
out of a comment" — is `_last_flag_value(_service_command_args(block), "--storage.tsdb.retention.time")`,
and `_strip_comments` is what makes the "not out of a comment" half true *by construction* instead of
by assertion.

One correction to carry into the clause. `_last_flag_value`'s docstring records that prom/prometheus
is **kingpin, which refuses a repeated flag** rather than picking one. Measured for this flag
(§5): a duplicated `--storage.tsdb.retention.time` is `Error parsing command line arguments: flag
'storage.tsdb.retention.time' cannot be repeated`, **rc=2**. So the `a482` first-vs-last class is
**LOUD** here, not silent — the row must not build a duplicate-detector arm and describe it as
closing a silence.

- `task-1786175232-d14e` (P3, the "re-serves this file verbatim" premise) is **not** armed: no

**`5c` is the highest-risk record in this queue and it survives the audit — no re-`ensure`.** It
was cut at `06a57e2` and has sat still through nine rounds across `5a` and `5b`, both of which moved
files under it. Every pointer in its acceptance criteria was executed or grepped at HEAD:

| What the record points at | At HEAD | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `prometheus.yml.j2:76` — "Prometheus REFUSES a config whose `scrape_timeout` **exceeds**" | :76, verbatim | green |
| `prometheus.yml.j2:50-60` — the pve `__param_target` triplet | :50-60 | green |

The two that moved are **stale, not false**: both criteria name a *symbol* that one grep finds, so
neither is unsatisfiable as written and neither costs a round. `ensure` reuses by key and *rewrites*
the record, so rewriting a 4 KB measured description to correct two line numbers is the larger risk
— the live figures ride in the `tasks.ready` payload instead, per `mem-1786141928-...`'s rule that
a re-`ensure` is licensed only by a false *contract*. What the payload must say loudly is that the
row's own body carries `:60` and `:1647`: this objective has already charged a row for shipping
stale line citations (`task-1786157581-f7a7`), so **copying either number into a guard comment is
the defect**, not the inheritance.

**One premise measured so `5c` does not spend a round on quoting**
(`logs/planner-step05c-target-spellings.py`, `.log`). All three of this row's targets CONCATENATE a
reference with a literal path suffix, and `prometheus.yml.j2:129-135` records that shape as a YAML
`ParserError` when unquoted. Driven at HEAD through the shipped `_neutralise_refs` +
`yaml.safe_load` and through `_LINE_BOUNDED_EXPR.fullmatch`, with the pve job's bare whole-value
target as the control:

| Target | bare | quoted |
|---|---|---|
| `{{ docker_host_plex_url }}/identity` | **`ParserError`** | OK |
| `{{ docker_host_plex_url }}/status/sessions` | **`ParserError`** | OK |
| `https://plex.{{ domain }}/identity` | **OK** | OK |
| control `{{ docker_host_pve_api_host }}` | OK | — |

All four are `fullmatch` against `_LINE_BOUNDED_EXPR`, so the membership predicate is not what
refuses them. **The discriminator is the value's FIRST character, not the concatenation** —
`_neutralise_refs` substitutes a SINGLE-QUOTED `'<REF>'`, so a value that *starts* with the
reference becomes a quoted scalar with trailing text and the parser stops; `https://plex.'<REF>'…`
is an ordinary plain scalar. So the generalisation a Builder would take from :129-135 — *"it is the
only one that CONCATENATES … so concatenation must be quoted"* — is **true of that line and false
as a rule**, and the proxied target is the counterexample this row adds to the file. Quote the two
that start with the reference because the gate's own reader needs it; if the proxied one is quoted
too it is for symmetry, and the comment must say that rather than repeating the false rule.
(None of this is Prometheus's opinion — nothing renders this template in the gate, as :133 says.)

**The three module names `5c`'s `module=` params must use, read off the document rather than off
prose**: `plex_identity_direct`, `plex_identity_proxied`, `plex_sessions`

take these values from the tuple and not from a comment.

`test_traefik_config_shape.py`), so leaving the tree dirty fuses two rows into one
unattributable commit and leaves every acceptance-1 in this wave — *"a RELATION read at this
row's own parent"* — with no parent to read. It also matters to the incident below: the working
tree **is** what got deployed, and an uncommitted tree makes the deployed state unnameable.

**The routing that cut `5b` was written from a premise that is measurably false**, filed as
`task-1786184652-2bdc` and handed to me by name at the finalization. `plan.md` premise 4 ordered

| | `Config.User` | `id -u` inside |
|---|---|---|
| `prom/prometheus:v3.12.0` | `nobody` | 65534 — true, and why `prometheus.yml` is `0644` |
| `prom/blackbox-exporter:v0.28.0` | **empty** | **0** |

So the crash-loop arm of the "collision" **does not exist for this file**, and `5a` had already
shipped `0640 root:root` with `BLACKBOX_MODE` pinning it — its own render comment says it chose
that mode in advance *"so that row changes a module and not a mode"*.

**And the door the row ordered is unsatisfiable in its second direction** — the part worth the
measurement (`logs/planner-step05b-owner-mode.log`). Five owner/mode pairs mounted `:ro` into the
pinned image, `65534:65534 0640` / `65534:65534 0600` / `root:root 0640` / `root:root 0600` /
`root:root 0644`: **`READ=ok` on all five**, because uid 0 bypasses the permission bits. There is
no pair in this row's space the process cannot read, so acceptance 4's *"mutating the owner back
to root REDS — the container could not read it"* orders a guard that would have to assert
something that never happens. That is the guard-rationale-disagrees-with-its-file defect this
objective has charged five times, and it would have been a round-1 rejection. Acceptance 4 now
arms **the world bit alone**, which is the invariant that survives: `0644` publishes a live Plex

**The incident (`task-1786190433-8514`, P1) does not park this loop and I did not treat it as

## Step 4 — closed but for its Demo (record retained in place)

### This was the first step that needed infrastructure which did not exist, and it did not park the loop

Steps 1–3 needed none, by `plan.md`'s own sequencing note. Step 4 needs a node-exporter process
inside CT 110 and a Prometheus job that can reach it. But the split is the same one Step 3 used:
the **renderer, the unit wiring, the role tasks, the scrape config and every guard are local and
gate-testable**, and only the *Demo* needs a deploy. So the wave is cut, the deploy is filed as
its own row (`4e`), and the loop advances. Parking behind an operator gate while seven numbered
steps are untouched is the failure this repo has already paid for twice — plex-monitoring
iterations 88–90, and two plex-ramdisk parks.

### Seven premises were measured before a row was written

They are appended to `plan.md`'s Step 4 in full and are the binding part of every row here. The
three that changed the shape of the wave:

1. **`plan.md`'s first Test names an instrument this repo does not have.** `prometheus_client`
   is importable under **neither** `.venv/bin/python` **nor** `mise exec -- python`
   (`ModuleNotFoundError`, both, measured at this cut), and `pyproject.toml` is
   `dependencies = []`. The obligation survives; the instrument becomes a stdlib parser that must
   itself be shown to **reject** four malformed inputs, or it is a second spelling of the writer —
   the charge DEC-243 laid on re-spelling a `that:` expression in Python.
2. **Trigger-time-only metrics would make the Demo un-runnable without a blip.** `.prom` written
   only from `capture_snapshot` leaves node-exporter re-serving one stale sample between stalls,
   and the operator manufacturing a stall to see the graph. `run()` already gets `None` from
   `read_line(timeout=0.5)` on every idle poll, so a **stat-only periodic refresh** is a few lines
   in a branch that exists — and adds no `_run_cmd` call, which is what keeps `5963` unarmed.
3. **The gate count is a RELATION in all five rows, never a literal.** It is **41** at `09bf3aa`
   (36 `scripts/test_*.py` + 3 tofu + 2 ansible). Three rows add a test file and their routing
   order is not fixed, so any row quoting `N` is a dead number before it is picked up. That
   defect forced a re-`ensure` at *both* Step 3 intra-wave cuts, and at the 3a→3c cut it had
   inverted 3c's criterion into one satisfiable at HEAD without writing a line. Every row here
   says instead: *run the gate at your own parent first, record `N`, the pass is `N` or `N+1`.*

### One filed row is PROMOTED, one is conditionally armed, one is a live fence — and one is deliberately left

- **`task-1786153086-9f13` is PROMOTED into row `4b`.** Step 11's clause 2 licenses it and the
  arming is measured rather than argued: the collector directory can only reach the watchdog
  through `templates/plex_blip_watchdog.service.j2:9`, that single `ExecStart` line already
  templates `plex_state_dir` into `--log-path` and does **not** into `--db-pattern`, and the row's
  whole fix is one more flag on the line `4b` must open anyway.
- **`task-1786154835-72f0` is conditionally armed by row `4c`.** `docs/runbooks/plex-sqlite-maintenance.md:37`
  offers *"`ansible/roles/plex/templates/` holds exactly one file (the watchdog unit)"* as
  **evidence** that this repo ships no maintenance timer. Measured at this cut: the directory holds
  exactly one file, and grepping the runbook's own shape guard for that clause returns **zero
  hits** — nothing re-runs it. A node-exporter drop-in carrying a role variable is idiomatically a
  second `.j2` in that directory, and landing one ships a false sentence in the document this loop
  corrected *for false sentences* eight commits ago. `4c` discharges the row if it adds the file.
- **`task-1786154819-f327` is a live fence on `4b` and `4c`, not a caution.** The runbook cites
  `tasks/main.yml:355-364`, `:366-424` and `:411-421`; every insertion above `:355` moves all
  three; and `f327` **measured** that the runbook's guard stays 10/10 through exactly that, because
  it range-checks rather than corresponds. Both rows must read the three citations back
  semantically after their edit and record that they did — the same by-hand read the Step 3c
  finalization performed.
- **`task-1786148835-48bd` is NOT promoted, and the reason is the honest one.** It is the highest-
  consequence row in the backlog — a `-readonly` open as root materialising root-owned sidecars,
  after which Plex serves reads and fails writes silently — but its own filing states that no plan
  step arms it, and Step 4 does not either. What Step 4 does is schedule another `just play`, which
  *runs* the audit. So row `4e` names it to the operator, in full, with the window stated (the
  seconds between systemd reporting `plexmediaserver` active and Plex opening the database), rather
  than being fixed here on an arming claim the evidence does not support. Its trigger stays: the
  first step that stops Plex around a play, which is **Step 10**.

Nothing else in Step 11 is armed, verified by census at this cut rather than read out of the
filings: no row here adds a `_run_cmd` call (`5963`), touches `_dry_run_fallback`'s guard (`658f`),
opens `scripts/analyze_plex_blips.py` (`65f2`), or edits `_wal_state` / the `.db` selection
(`e85d`, `ffe0`, `55f9`). **`99cf` has a tripwire rather than a verdict**: `4a` is forbidden to
read `plex_blip_diagnostics_*.jsonl` back, and if a Builder decides counter continuity across
restarts requires it, `99cf` arms and promotes into that row instead of being stepped over.

### Step 1's state is unchanged, and stated again so it is not read as either done or blocked

Step 1 is **code complete and on `main`**; its **Demo is pending an operator deploy**
(`task-1786133422-3791`, P1, operator-only). All three rows are closed. Row `4e` records the one
fact that connects them: **`plex_watchdog_probe_status{probe="fuser"}` reads 1 only after Step 1's
`psmisc` install is actually deployed**, so a single `just play` closes both gates — worth saying
to the operator rather than asking for two deploys. That is now the second time this loop has
paired gates onto one play (Step 3 did it with `b999`), and it is why neither gate blocks a step.

### Step 4's wave, as it was cut and as it ran

Five rows. Keys are `code-assist:plex-blip-manual-triage:step-04:{slug}`. **Four closed; `4e` is
the operator Demo and remains open.**

| Row | Key slug | Task ID | P | Blocked by | Owns |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4a | `watchdog-textfile-metrics` | `task-1786156188-0d90` **CLOSED `6a23d58`** | 2 | — | `ansible/roles/plex/files/plex_blip_watchdog.py`: the renderer, the atomic writer, `--textfile-dir`, the idle-branch gauge refresh — and `scripts/test_plex_blip_watchdog.py` |
| 4b | `watchdog-metrics-wiring` | `task-1786156188-4920` **CLOSED `5bd25eb`** | 2 | ~~4a~~ cleared | `templates/plex_blip_watchdog.service.j2:9`, the collector-dir tasks, `defaults/main.yml`, a new unit shape test — **and `task-1786153086-9f13`, promoted and CLOSED with it** |
| 4c | `node-exporter-ct110` | `task-1786156188-24d6` **CLOSED `a0915e9`** | 2 | — | node-exporter install + restrained unit in `tasks/main.yml`/`defaults/main.yml`, a new shape test — **and `task-1786154835-72f0` and `task-1786159051-cb29`, both promoted and both CLOSED with it** |
| 4d | `prometheus-scrape-job` | `task-1786156188-5ee9` **CLOSED `06a57e2`** | 3 | ~~4c~~ cleared | `ansible/roles/docker_host/templates/prometheus.yml.j2` + `defaults/main.yml`: the 15 s job, port pinned by relation — and the guard is an EXTENSION of `scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py`, not a new file |
| 4e | `operator-demo-grafana` | `task-1786156188-753c` | 1 | 4a,4b,4c,4d | OPERATOR GATE: `just play`, then `plex_sqlite_wal_bytes` in Grafana at its true value |

**Routed first: 4a**, and it is dependency as much as scheduling. `4b` cannot name a flag
`argparse` does not define — a unit that passes an unknown option does not warn, it exits
non-zero and systemd restarts it forever, which is the false-contract class this loop has charged
three times (`playbooks/plex.yml`, a task name that did not exist, a 90-character CI path behind a
quoted literal). `4a` is also the only row that is **entirely** provable in-loop through the real
CLI, and the one Steps 5–9 read.

**`4c` is ready in parallel and was deliberately not routed.** It is independent of `4a` (different
file, different language) and the Builder takes one task at a time; leaving it ready rather than
blocked means the next routing has a choice that does not depend on how `4a` went.

**Two blockers in this wave are false-contract blockers, not tidiness.** `4b` on `4a` for the flag
above; `4d` on `4c` because a scrape job naming a port or path no unit binds reads **UP** in
Status → Targets and produces **zero series** — the failure already recorded, from measurement, in
`prometheus.yml.j2:31-42`'s pve-exporter comment. `4d`'s guard therefore reads the listen address
out of what `4c` ships instead of typing `9100` twice.

**What no row in this wave may do**, so the fence is one place rather than five: open
`scripts/analyze_plex_blips.py`, edit `_wal_state` or the `base_dbs` selection, touch the WAL audit
at `tasks/main.yml:355-424`, or modify `scripts/test_plex_wal_guard_shape.py`. Measured at this
cut and worth knowing before someone worries about it: `_lifted_tasks()` in that guard lifts the
**stat and assert** tasks only, **not** the journal-mode audit — so the audit is reachable by a
later row without disturbing the behavioural harness.

## Step 4 intra-wave routing — `4a` closed, **`4b` routed** (2026-08-10, parent `6a23d58`)

`4a` closed at `6a23d58`; gate 41/41 and suite 92/92 were re-run by the Finalizer, and four rows
were filed off it. Two rows of this wave were ready — `4b` and `4c` — and **`4b` is routed**.

### `4c` is the critical-path row and I did NOT route it, which is the judgement worth reading

`4d` blocks on `4c` alone and `4e` on all four, so routing `4c` first would shorten the chain by an
iteration. It is rejected because it manufactures a **false contract**, which is the class this loop
has already re-`ensure`d for twice. `4c`'s own text says its
`--collector.textfile.directory` must point at *"the same variable **4b** writes into"*, and `4b`'s
OWNS clause assigns `defaults/main.yml` to `4b`. Run `4c` first and the Builder must either invent
that variable — making `4b`'s OWNS false the moment it is picked up — or type a literal, which is
**`9f13` in a second container**: two strings that agree by coincidence, in the one wave whose
declared purpose is removing that coincidence. One iteration of latency is the cheaper side.

`4b` is also the row that **discharges `9f13`**, the only backlog row a Step 4 row is contractually
obliged to close, and its blocker is genuinely gone: `--textfile-dir` is at
`plex_blip_watchdog.py:921` and `--db-pattern` at `:917`, both measured at `6a23d58`.

### The re-`ensure` — `4b`'s arming clause named an instrument the gate cannot run

The row asked for *"a rendered-template equality"*. Measured this turn: **`jinja2` is
`ModuleNotFoundError` under both `python3` and `.venv/bin/python`**, and `run_gate.py:54` runs every
`scripts/test_*.py` under `sys.executable`. So the row's arming instrument **cannot be a gate test** —
a dead harness in the acceptance criteria, which is the one thing that licenses rewriting the record
(`mem-…-reensure-only-for-false-contracts`). It is the same instrument correction `4d`'s acceptance 4
already carries; `4b` was written before that measurement generalised.

The obligation is unchanged and the instrument is corrected in two directions:

- **The guard proves a REFERENCE CHAIN in raw text** — ExecStart must carry `{{ plex_library_db }}`
  itself, not its expansion, and `plex_library_db` must derive from `plex_state_dir`. That is
  strictly stronger against `9f13`'s actual defect than a render would be: `9f13` is *two matching
  literals*, and pinning the reference kills the literal directly. "An override moves both halves
  together" becomes a theorem about the chain rather than an experiment.
- **Rendering survives as the by-hand Demo.** `ansible-playbook` is on PATH via mise and reports
  **jinja 3.1.6** in its own venv. Acceptance 6 now names that interpreter instead of implying the
  gate's.

Acceptance 2 gained the mutant that actually tests the discharge: **the `{{ plex_library_db }}`
reference replaced by the literal path it expands to** — `9f13` restored by hand. A guard green
through that has not closed the row.

The coincidence was re-measured rather than quoted: `defaults/main.yml:46`'s `plex_library_db` is
byte-for-byte argparse's `--db-pattern` default (`:917`) minus the trailing `*`.

### Arming census for the four rows `4a` filed — measured, not read out of the filings

- **`task-1786159051-cb29`** (P2, the `.prom` name contract) — **armed by `4c`, not by `4b`.** The
  relation is between `TEXTFILE_NAME` and node-exporter's `*.prom` glob, and *the glob does not
  exist in this repo until `4c` lands the collector*. A guard written at `4b` would have to type
  `.prom` as its second end, which is the second-spelling defect. Promote it at the `4c` routing.
- **`task-1786159059-e3bc`** (P3, `parse_exposition` over-strict) — **armed by `4c`.** Its own
  filing says it must land *before* `4c`, because `4c`'s shape test is the first thing to feed that
  parser a document this repo did not write. `4b` renders a systemd unit, not exposition.
- **`task-1786159059-184b`** (P3, the 15.0 s refresh default) — **homeless in this wave**, and this
  is a measurement rather than a deferral. `textfile_refresh_sec` is a **constructor kwarg with no
  CLI flag**: `main()` (`:924-933`) passes `log_path`, `output_dir`, `db_pattern`, `debounce_sec`,
  `threshold_ms`, `dry_run`, `textfile_dir` — and not it. So there is no flag for `4b` to template
  and no role variable for `4d` to read; making it a relation needs an `add_argument`, which is
  Python in `files/` — outside `4b`'s fence *and* outside `4d`'s (`nothing under roles/plex/`). It
  stays a Step 11 row until a step reopens the module.
- **`task-1786159059-f653`** (P3, vacuous dry-run guard) — not armed: it lives in the Python and in
  `test_plex_blip_watchdog.py`, both outside `4b`'s fence.
- **`task-1786159639-39db`** (P3, restart drops four families) — correctly routed to **Step 7**
  (`plan.md:399`, alert rules) by the Finalizer. No Step 4 row owns it. Left there.
- **`task-1786157581-f7a7`** (P3, stale line citations) — **not armed by `4b`**, and it got worse.
  Both citing files are outside the row's ownership, and acceptance 3 explicitly forbids opening

`plan.md`'s Step 4 checklist marker stays `[~]`: one row of five is closed, four remain. Step 1

## Step 4 intra-wave routing — `4b` closed, **`4c` routed** (2026-08-11, parent `5bd25eb`)

`4b` closed at `5bd25eb` and took `task-1786153086-9f13` with it — the only backlog row a Step 4
row was contractually obliged to discharge. **`4c` is the only ready non-operator row left in this
wave** (`4d` blocks on `4c` alone, `4e` on all four), so there is no routing choice to make. The
judgement in this turn is entirely about the *record*, and it is a re-`ensure`.

### THE RE-`ensure` — three of this row's own citations went stale inside `4b`'s commit

`4b` inserted 14 lines above the WAL audit. That moved every `path:LINE` this row carried, and the
row's FENCE was one of them:

| Where in the record | Said | True at `5bd25eb` |
|---|---|---|
| FENCE, the WAL audit | `tasks/main.yml:355-424` | the three audit tasks are `:369-438` |
| Acceptance 4, the runbook's citations | `:355-364`, `:366-424`, `:411-421` | `:369-378`, `:380-438`, `:425-435` |
| "What to build", the drop-in idiom | `:311-321` | the override-dir + `LIBVA_DRIVER_NAME` pair at `:325-343` |

The fence one is the material one and it is wrong in **both** directions: `:355-424` covers
`:355-368`, which is the *watchdog service* task the row may legitimately need to read, and it
**stops short of `:425-438`**, the assert's `fail_msg` — the exact lines the runbook cites and
`Step 3b` shipped. A Builder obeying that fence literally is licensed to edit the one region the
fence exists to protect. That is a false contract in the acceptance criteria, which is the single
thing that licenses rewriting a record
(`mem-…-reensure-only-for-false-contracts`), and the record is otherwise preserved sentence for
sentence.

**The replacement is an ANCHOR, not a fresh set of numbers**, because this loop has now measured
twice that corrected line numbers die faster than the row that carries them
(`mem-1786160126-1525`: `f7a7`'s own replacements were dead one commit after it was filed). The
fence now names the three audit tasks by their `- name:` strings and tells the Builder to grep.
Acceptance 4 stopped naming ranges altogether and names a **method** instead: read the citations
out of the runbook at your own parent, and score them by **content equality parent-vs-HEAD** with
each region asserted non-empty and its line count printed — the Step 4b finalization's own zsh
harness printed three `IDENTICAL`s over two empty strings (`mem-1786165292-8ccb`), so the method
carries its own harness-safety rule.

### `cb29` is PROMOTED, and the arming is a measurement rather than a plan

`task-1786159051-cb29` (P2) — `TEXTFILE_NAME` → `plex_blip.metrics` leaves the suite 92/92 green
while node-exporter globs `*.prom` only. The 4a→4b routing predicted this row would arm at `4c`
*"because the glob does not exist in this repo until 4c lands the collector"*, and that holds: this
is the first row that configures a textfile collector at all. Composed with `mem-1786159649-6e78`
the consequence is worse than a missing file — an **absent** series cannot match design 5.3's
`==`/`!=` alert expressions, so a mis-named collector file is silent by construction.

The trap is named in the row rather than left for a Critic to find: pinning the suffix by typing
`.prom` a second time in the role is **two literals that agree**, in the wave whose declared purpose
was deleting exactly that. Where the only honest second end is upstream behaviour, the row says to
*say so in the docstring* and pin the literal with its rationale — an upstream constant has no
in-repo second end. The acceptance is the mutant either way, and the guard must **read** the name
out of the module (which this row may read, and may not edit).

### `e3bc` is NOT armed by `4c`, and the previous routing's note is corrected

The 4a→4b routing recorded `task-1786159059-e3bc` (parse_exposition over-strict) as *"armed by 4c,
because 4c's shape test is the first thing to feed that parser a document this repo did not
write."* **Measured this turn: that is false.** `parse_exposition` exists in exactly one file,

ships an Ansible unit and **no exposition document**; and node-exporter cannot run on this box, so

output. The correction is written into `4c`'s record too, so the prediction is not inherited a
third time.

### What `4b` left standing, so `4c` asserts an equality instead of inventing a variable

Measured at `5bd25eb`, not read out of `4b`'s record: `defaults/main.yml` carries
`plex_watchdog_textfile_dir`, the `ExecStart` passes `--textfile-dir "{{ plex_watchdog_textfile_dir }}"`,

## Step 4 intra-wave routing — `4c` closed, **`4d` routed** (2026-08-11, parent `a0915e9`)

`4c` closed at `a0915e9` and took **both** of its promoted backlog rows with it (`72f0` and `cb29`,
each re-driven by hand at the finalization). **`4d` is the only ready non-operator row left in this
wave** — `4e` is the operator Demo and blocks on `4d` — so, exactly as at the last cut, there is no
routing *choice*. The judgement in this turn is again about the *record*, and it is again a
re-`ensure`; this time the false clause is not a stale line number but a **census that never
happened**.

### THE RE-`ensure` — the row told the Builder that the instrument it needs does not exist

`4d`'s record carried, as a measured parenthetical: *"census `scripts/` first — there is no
prometheus scrape shape test today, the nearest neighbour is `test_grafana_provisioning_shape.py`."*
**Both clauses are false at `a0915e9`**, and the second one is false in the direction that costs a
round:

| The row said | Measured this turn |
|---|---|
| no prometheus scrape shape test exists | `scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py:49` binds `PROM_SCRAPE = TEMPLATES / "prometheus.yml.j2"` and reads it at four sites (`:1454`, `:2315`, `:3572`, `:4016`) |
| nearest neighbour is `test_grafana_provisioning_shape.py` | that file's `prometheus` hits are the *datasource*; the true neighbour ships **two scrape-job guards** — `pve-exporter` (multi-target) and `plex-exporter` (single-target, interval/timeout pair, `no_multi_target_shape`) |

A Builder following that literally opens a new file and re-spells `_scrape_job_block`,
`_indented_block`/`_indented_blocks`, `_block_scalar`, `_scrape_target_port`, `_yaml_unquote` and
`_duration_seconds` — **a second parser for one artifact**, which is `9f13`'s class one level up,
and each of those readers carries a correction that was bought at measurement. The `_indented_blocks`
PLURAL is the one to know: reading only the first `- targets:` printed `target_is_pve_host=True` over
a `static_configs` list that loaded a caller-chosen host with the homelab's PVE credential. It also
moves acceptance 1's arm (`N` vs `N+1`), so the false clause reaches the acceptance and not only the
prose. That is the false-contract trigger; every measured sentence of the original row is preserved
and only the census clause is replaced. `mem-1786168582-db58`.

### The spelling was decided by a guard the row never mentioned, and I measured it rather than predicted it

`prometheus.yml.j2` sits in that same guard's `LINE_READ_TEMPLATES` (`:2315`), so every `{{ }}` it
carries must `fullmatch` `_LINE_BOUNDED_EXPR` (`:2296`) — a **bare reference** with at most
`| default(<literal>)`. Run against the real compiled regex at this parent:

| Candidate for the target host | `fullmatch` |
|---|---|
| `hostvars['plex'].ansible_host` | **True** |
| `hostvars['plex']['ansible_host']` | **True** |
| `hostvars[groups['plex'] \| first].ansible_host` | **False** — *"not a bare reference"* |
| `hostvars[groups['plex'][0]].ansible_host` | **False** |

So the two candidates trade against each other and neither is free. The `groups[…]` form is the one
robust to a rename — `scripts/gen_inventory.py` writes the GROUP name `plex` as structure (`:74`) and
takes the HOST name from tofu's `plex_name` output (`:76`) — and it is **refused by a guard already in
the gate**. The accepted form names the host as a literal. The row therefore routes the accepted
spelling with the weakness discharged **by relation** rather than by comment: the guard asserts
`ansible/inventory/hosts.yml` really carries a host of that name under group `plex`, so a tofu rename
reds the gate instead of pointing the job at nothing. Handing the Builder this table is the whole
value of the turn — it is a round-2 rejection converted into a precondition. `mem-1786168582-c46a`.

### The port relation is real, and the half that cannot be rendered is named

Measured, not read out of `4c`'s record: `plex_node_exporter_listen_address: 0.0.0.0:9100`
(`roles/plex/defaults/main.yml:88`) is **live** — the drop-in
`templates/prometheus_node_exporter_override.conf.j2` passes it as
`--web.listen-address={{ plex_node_exporter_listen_address }}`. So `4d`'s "port pinned by relation to
what the plex role ships" has something real on the other end.

But `ansible/site.yml` runs `docker_host` and `plex` as **separate plays**, so a `plex` role default
is not in scope where this template renders. The relation lives in the **guard**, not in the Jinja,
and the row now says so — a Builder who assumes otherwise writes a `{{ plex_node_exporter_… }}` that
renders empty. Two consequences are written in: **split the address** (comparing `0.0.0.0:9100` whole
against a scrape target can only ever be red — the PORT is the half that relates), and **do not let
both ends resolve to the same variable**, which is `task-1786167365-e571`, this wave's freshest
filing, in a row that has the same shape available to it.

### Arming census for `4c`'s four filings — none is armed by `4d`, and one is armed *as a class*

`c0e3`, `d389`, `e571` and `1152` all have their subject under `ansible/roles/plex/` or in
`scripts/test_plex_node_exporter_shape.py`, which is **exactly `4d`'s fence**. Measured against the
row's OWNS clause rather than by topic — the instrument `mem-1786165716-3ad7` prescribes after the
`e3bc` prediction propagated twice. `e571` is nevertheless quoted **inside** `4d`'s record, because
the tautology it names is a shape `4d`'s own by-relation guard could reproduce; a filing can be
out of reach and still be the right warning.

Two things `4d` **inherits rather than builds**, so no round is spent re-wiring them: the render task
already carries `notify: Restart prometheus`, and that notify *and its handler* are already pinned by
`test_traefik_config_shape.py:3413-3491` (prometheus.yml is a read-only bind mount parsed at start,
no `--web.enable-lifecycle`). This is `d389`'s subject already closed in the other role — worth
recording as the contrast rather than left as an absence.

### The parse proof was measured so it is not discovered in round 1

Acceptance 4 of the original row promised the rendered YAML "is proven to PARSE" and noted PyYAML is
importable. Both halves check out, and the composition does not: **PyYAML 6.0.3 imports under
`.venv/bin/python`, and `yaml.safe_load` on `prometheus.yml.j2` RAISES today** (`ConstructorError` —
`{{ docker_host_pve_api_host }}` reads as a flow mapping), while `jinja2` is `ModuleNotFoundError`
under that same interpreter and `ansible-playbook` reports core 2.21.1 with jinja 3.1.6 in its own
venv. So the parse proof is a **by-hand Demo** (4b acceptance 6's precedent) or a refusing
mini-renderer proven byte-equal out of band (`4c`'s, `mem-1786166903-601a`) — never a gate test. The
obligation is unchanged; only the instrument is named, which is the same correction this loop has now
made at three consecutive cuts.

### One shape fact from the file itself

`job_name: node-exporter` is **already taken** (`prometheus.yml.j2:16`) by the docker host's own
compose-network exporter. Two jobs emitting `node_*` families are tellable apart only by their
labels, and the new job needs its own name — cheap to say here, invisible until a dashboard is empty.

`plan.md`'s Step 4 marker stays `[~]` and its note is reconciled: three rows of five closed, `4d`
routed, `4e` the operator Demo. Step 1 stays `[~]`.

### Measured at the Step 1 delivered tree (`5c9a830`)

Four things were measured at the delivered tree (`5c9a830`) before the wave was cut,
rather than carried from `plan.md`. Full detail in `context.md`; the load-bearing ones:

1. **`psmisc` is genuinely absent**, and `plan.md`'s "the package list in
   `ansible/roles/plex/tasks/main.yml`" is **ambiguous** — that file holds two apt name
   lists (`plex_media_packages` at :38, the inline watchdog-deps list at :237-245).
   `psmisc` belongs in the second. Corrected in 1c's acceptance criteria, with a
   negative assertion, because the wrong placement still installs and still looks green.

2. **`_run_cmd` has no clock at all** (`:82-96`). `elapsed_ms` is not unreported, it is
   unmeasured — so a test asserting only `status == "timeout"` would pass against the
   pre-change code for the wrong reason. 1a's acceptance therefore asserts the
   **number**. `FileNotFoundError` also falls into the generic `except`, so `missing`
   and `error` are one string today; the four-status contract is a real refinement.

3. **A casualty `plan.md` does not name.** Three dry-run branches (`:335`, `:337`,
   `:343`) test `_run_cmd`'s return **as a string** (`"probe error" in fuser_out`).
   Against a `dict` that tests *keys* and is always `False`. Not a crash — the dry-run
   fallbacks **silently stop firing**, which survives a green test run. This is a
   functional dependency on the return *shape*, so grepping `_run_cmd` finds the call
   sites but not the risk. Given to 1b as its own row with an anti-vacuity test that
   goes red on a naive dict conversion.

4. **The existing suite is barely coupled to the shape** — `test_plex_blip_watchdog.py`
   touches it at exactly one line (`:137 assertIn("lock_holders", record)`), asserting
   the key exists and never its type. So `plan.md`'s "existing tests still pass
   unmodified" is a checkable contract, and a Builder editing an existing assertion has
   changed something Step 1 did not ask for.

`ansible-playbook --syntax-check site.yml` from `ansible/`. *(Corrected 2026-08-08: this

## Row 1a — `run-cmd-structured-result` (`task-1786120197-5507`), built

`_run_cmd` at `plex_blip_watchdog.py:82` now returns design §5.1's
`{status, elapsed_ms, output, error}`. Default timeout `0.5 → 2.0`, still overridable
on the same kwarg. `time.monotonic()` is read once before `subprocess.run` and the
elapsed value is computed **in every branch including `TimeoutExpired`** — the old
function had no clock at all, so this number did not previously exist to be lost.
`FileNotFoundError` is caught **before** the generic handler, which is what makes
`missing` reachable at all.

**All four spellings, with the input that produces each** (acceptance 2), each covered
by a test and jointly pinned as a set by
`test_run_cmd_status_vocabulary_is_exactly_four_spellings`:

| status | input |
|---|---|
| `ok` | `python -c print(...)` — exits normally |
| `timeout` | `python -c "import time; time.sleep(5)"` at `timeout=0.2` |
| `missing` | `plex-blip-no-such-binary-xyz` — not on PATH (the no-`psmisc` `fuser` case) |
| `error` | a `0o644` file that exists but is not executable → `PermissionError` |

### Verification

- **RED** — `logs/red-step01a-run-cmd.log`: `Ran 6 tests … FAILED (failures=5,
  errors=1)`, written and run *before* the source change. Five fail at
  `assertIsInstance(res, dict)` against the bare strings (`'[probe timeout exceeded]'`,
  `'[probe error: [Errno 2] …]'`, `'[probe error: [Errno 13] …]'`, the stdout string);
  the sixth is the default-timeout pin (`0.5 != 2.0`). The timeout row asserts the
  **number**, not just the status, per the Planner's note.
- **GREEN** — `logs/green-step01a-run-cmd.log`: `Ran 6 tests … OK`.
- **Existing suite unmodified** (acceptance 3): `TestPlexBlipWatchdog` 8/8 OK, and
  `git diff --numstat scripts/test_plex_blip_watchdog.py` = `107 0` — **zero deleted
  lines**, so no existing assertion (including `:137 assertIn("lock_holders", record)`)
  was touched. The new tests are a separate `TestRunCmdStructuredResult` class.
- **`just test`** (acceptance 4): `GATE PASS: 38/38 steps` — `logs/gate-step01a.log`.
  The gate runs this suite at line 558.
- **Real-app smoke** (not required by the row; run because the change is deployed code):
  drove the actual script with `--dry-run --max-triggers 1` against a live `TX_STALL`
  line. JSONL round-trips, all eight top-level keys retained, and `pidstat` came back
  `{"status": "missing", "elapsed_ms": 0.24, …}` — the new spelling doing its job on a
  box without `sysstat`.

### Step 1a rework — DEC-208 rejection: the header contradicted the new default

**Active task:** `task-1786120197-5507` / `code-assist:plex-blip-manual-triage:step-01:run-cmd-structured-result`

The Critic rejected the increment for a **contradiction, not a falsehood**. `:7` advertised
`executes instant diagnostic snapshots (<500ms)` while the `_run_cmd` docstring 83 lines
below named `0.5s` as the *retired* number. Pre-text both sentences were wrong but coherent;
delivered, one file argued with itself about one figure.

**The measurement, not a grep.** `logs/probe_bound_harness.py` writes `sleep 30` stubs named
`fuser`/`lsof`/`pidstat`/`ps` into a tempdir, prepends it to `PATH`, and times the *real*
`capture_snapshot`. Container-free, ~8s per sha. Run at all three
(`logs/probe-bound-measurement.txt`):

| sha | measured | vs. the claim in its own header |
|---|---|---|
| parent `5c9a830` | 2010.29 ms | **4.02x** the 500ms claim |
| rejected `77c56d0` | 8015.71 ms | **16.03x** the 500ms claim |
| repaired (working) | 8013.90 ms | **1.00x** the new 8000ms claim |

Row 1 is the attribution and the reason this was a rejection rather than a filed row: the
sentence was **already false** before this round touched anything, so the round is not charged
with creating it — only with creating the disagreement. Row 3 is the repair landing: the
header is now true, the 0.17% over budget being four `subprocess` spawns, not slack.

**The repair** (per the standing flip-the-polarity rule — replace a dead guard with a stronger
true claim, never delete it): `:7` now reads `executes bounded diagnostic snapshots (2.0s
default per probe, four probes serial, so 8.0s worst case)`, plus a sentence naming *why* a
blocking probe is allowed to burn its timeout. The still-true `>500ms` slow-query threshold on
`:6` is a different figure and was left alone.

**Two new tests** (`TestModuleLatencyBoundIsTrue`) pin the header to the **code**, not to a
literal: the probe count is counted out of the source and the timeout read off the signature,
so a fifth probe or a new default forces the header to move in the same commit. RED 2/2 before
(`'2.0s' not found in ...`), GREEN 2/2 after.

**Verification:** full suite **16/16** (14 prior + 2 new). `git diff --numstat` on the test
file = `45 0` — **zero deletions**, existing assertions untouched. `just test` **GATE PASS
38/38**. Repair proven **inert** by AST comparison against `77c56d0` with all docstrings
stripped — *identical*, so this is a documentation-only change and the 1a contract that passed
review is byte-for-byte intact. Runtime unchanged across the repair (8015.71 → 8013.90 ms)
independently confirms it.

The scope fence still holds: `capture_snapshot` is **not** rewired, and 1b
(`task-1786120230-df98`) still owns the three stale dry-run branches.

### Step 1a round 3 — DEC-210 consumed: the budget WORD, and the bound the guard never pinned

DEC-210 rejected round 2 on two things, both small, and neither of them the implementation
(the Critic re-derived 16/16, gate 38/38, `45 0` numstat, and the AST inertness claim at their
own hands, and said so). Both are repaired here.

**(1) The word half.** Round 2 swept the retired *number* at `:7` and added `:10` "The snapshot
is bounded, not instant" — but 358 lines down, `capture_snapshot`'s own docstring at `:368`
still read `Execute instantaneous diagnostic probes`. That is the function whose 8016 ms the
header now advertises, so the file argued with itself about one figure. Note the general shape,
because it is counterintuitive: *negating* a budget word in prose is strictly more dangerous
than leaving the header vague — it converts every surviving copy of the word from quietly stale
into a live self-contradiction. `:368` now names the bounded behaviour and quotes **no figure at
all**; the header owns the number, and a figure quoted far from the code it describes is exactly
how `<500ms` went stale in the first place. Full-file census: the only surviving `instant` in the
module is `:10`'s own negation. `Zero-overhead` at `:2`/`:452` is the inotify loop and stays —
same separator that spared `:6`.

**(2) The bound half.** `TestModuleLatencyBoundIsTrue` pinned the two *inputs* to the bound (the
2.0s timeout, the "four probes serial" count) and never their **product** — the one number an
operator actually reads. Measured by the Critic: deleting `, so 8.0s worst case` from the header
outright was GREEN 16/16. The new assertion derives `probes * default` from the source and
requires the header to name it, so it is the same derived-not-hardcoded shape as the other two.

**RED first, and the guard caught its own author:** the census test failed on exactly `:368` and
nothing else. The first replacement docstring was worded "Bounded, **not instantaneous**" — the
new guard immediately failed on that too, which is the guard working, so the sentence was
reworded rather than the guard weakened.

**Verification.** Suite **18/18** (16 prior + 2 new). `git diff --numstat`: test file `49 0` —
**zero deletions** again. `just test` **GATE PASS 38/38**.
The Critic's own mutation harness, run unmodified (`logs/builder-5507-r3-mutation.txt`), now
scores **A2 and B2 RED** where they were GREEN at `3bfdb7b` — the instrument that found the hole
reports it closed. `logs/builder-5507-r3-guard-closes.py` adds the Critic's headline case they
had not scripted (delete the bound sentence outright) and reports the **failing test name** per
case, not a bare rc: C → product test only, D → census test only, E → both. Each hole is closed
by its own assertion, with no cross-catching and no vacuity.
Inertness re-proven and extended to a chain (`logs/builder-5507-r3-inert.py`): docstring-stripped

  routed on stale prose; it is being outranked.
- Left visibly ready on purpose. That is the signal that makes the next Planner price it,
  and routing is decided by the `tasks.ready` event, not by the ready set. Re-blocking it
  onto 1b or 1c to keep the set tidy would be churn against a record with nothing at risk.

#### The trap in 3c, measured at this cut: the obvious correction reds 3b's shipped guard

3b's `fail_msg` points at this runbook, and `test_plex_wal_guard_shape.py` reads the path back out
of the message and holds the file to the promise. Its **arming** probe reconstructs the parent's

and that catch survives only while the runbook **lacks** the token. `procedure` is already present
as a substring of `procedures` at `:3`, so `wal_checkpoint` is the whole of the arming.

Differential run, control-before / mutant / restore / control-after, `-B` to defeat stale bytecode:

    control  (runbook untouched)                       -> PASS 14/14, probes_caught {remedy:1, missing-file:1}
    mutant   (append a fenced PRAGMA wal_checkpoint …) -> FAIL  1/14, probes_caught {remedy:0, missing-file:1}
    restore  sha256 5ebf1ccf82c4… byte-identical
    control  after restore                             -> PASS 14/14

So the single most natural thing 3c could do — document the checkpoint remedy the guard's own
message spells out — silently takes the gate down, and the failure lands in **another row's file**
where a Builder reading their own green shape test would not look. Charged to 3c rather than filed:
it has a home, and the row is now **licensed** to re-arm that one probe with a claim word the
runbook genuinely lacks (flip it, never delete it), or to take the other door and keep the token
out. Either door, the row must report `test_plex_wal_guard_shape.py`'s count explicitly — a silent
14 → 1 is the whole failure mode.

lowercase tokens `journal_mode` and `audit` (`_pointer_defects` requires every claim word ≥ 4

### The measurement that decided the wave — the guard's red is reproducible WITHOUT a deploy

`plan.md`'s Step 3 Demo is `just play`, which is operator-only. Whether the *step* is closable by
the loop therefore turns on one question nobody had asked: can the guard be watched going red
without CT 110? Measured at this cut rather than reasoned about.

`logs/planner-step3-wal-guard-probe.yml` (saved; `logs/` is gitignored), driven with

```
mise exec -- ansible-playbook -i localhost, <probe>.yml \
  -e "plex_state_dir=<tmp> plex_wal_max_bytes=8388608"
```

against a real `journal_mode=wal` database with a `truncate`d `-wal`:

- **RED** at **43,735,168** — `fatal`, `"assertion": "not plex_wal.stat.exists or plex_wal.stat.size < plex_wal_max_bytes | int"`
- **GREEN** at **2,097,152** — `ok=2 changed=0 failed=0`

No `sqlite3`, no CT 110, no deploy. So Step 3 closes in-loop and `just play` is a gate on the
step's *live* evidence, not on its correctness — the same split Step 1 already uses.

### The wave's premises, measured at the cut rather than carried from `plan.md`

Per the standing rule — execute every harness a routed row's criteria cite and grep every name,
file and line they point at. Everything below was read or run at this turn.

1. **`plan.md:152` is wrong about the mechanism, in the direction that costs a round.** It says
   "Today's audit asserts `journal_mode == 'wal'`". It is **not** an `ansible.builtin.assert`:
   `tasks/main.yml:344-352` is `ansible.builtin.command` + `failed_when: plex_sqlite_wal.stdout
   != 'wal'`, named *"Verify Plex SQLite DB is in WAL mode (read-only audit)"*. A Builder
   grepping the plex role for `assert` finds the **iHD/QSV gate at :364** instead and edits the
   wrong task. Stated in 3b.

2. **`sqlite3` is NOT installed on the dev box**, so today's audit cannot be exercised locally at
   all — it fails `rc=2, "sqlite3: command not found"`, *not* on WAL size. This is why 3b's guard
   must be **split**, with the size half standing on `ansible.builtin.stat` alone. A condition
   that hangs the size check off the registered `sqlite3` output is undemonstrable in-loop and
   pushes the entire Demo behind the operator gate.

3. **`jinja2` is importable under NEITHER `.venv/bin/python` NOR `mise exec -- python`.** It
   exists only inside the version-pinned pipx path
   `…/pipx-ansible-core/2.21.1/venvs/ansible-core/bin/python` (3.1.6), which is a rot bomb to
   hardcode. PyYAML *is* importable under the gate interpreter. So a shape test **cannot** render
   the guard's `that:` expression — it must drive `ansible-playbook` itself. A Python
   re-implementation of the condition is a second spelling of the thing under test and cannot
   fail with it.

4. **`plex_wal_max_bytes` appears ZERO times in `ansible/`** today. `plex_state_dir` lives at
   `defaults/main.yml:40`, which is the new variable's home.

5. **`db_pattern` already matches all three files.** `plex_blip_watchdog.py:433` is
   `…com.plexapp.plugins.library.db*` and `capture_snapshot:470-471` already resolves it via
   `glob.glob`. 3a reuses that resolution; a second hardcoded path is a second spelling, which
   the record's own comment at `:502` already forbids for `hold_site`.

6. **The key-set guard is the one test line Step 3 is licensed to modify, and the licence is the
   row, not the guard.** `test_plex_blip_watchdog.py:462` defines `TOP_LEVEL_KEYS`, `:691`
   asserts it by equality as *"the ten after Step 2"*, and its own message says *"Step 3 adds
   sqlite — an ADDITION here is expected"*. That message is **not** a flip licence; 3a's
   acceptance is, and it says so in writing. The removal half at `:699` is a **subset** check and
   is green on both sides of the commit, so red→green is no evidence for it — 3a must score it by
   mutation instead. `:1162` also reads `TOP_LEVEL_KEYS`.

7. **GATE ARITHMETIC INVERTS INSIDE THIS WAVE, so each row states its own reading.**
   `run_gate.py:53` globs `scripts/test_*.py`: today **34** shape + 3 tofu + 2 ansible = **39**.
   3a and 3b **extend existing files**, so an unchanged **39/39 is the pass**. 3c **adds
   `scripts/test_plex_sqlite_maintenance_runbook_shape.py`**, so its pass is **40/40** and an
   unchanged 39 means the new file was never collected — a vacuous green. Both readings are in
   the row records so the number is not misread in either direction.

8. **The runbook carries a SECOND falsehood, and a shape test pinning only the first would
   declare the file correct.** `plex-sqlite-maintenance.md:25` says *"Plex Media Server has a
   scheduled task that performs `VACUUM` and `REINDEX` … configured to run at 03:30 AM."* Grep of
   `ansible/` (excluding `galaxy_roles`) for `VACUUM|REINDEX|03:30`: **zero hits**;
   `roles/plex/templates/` holds exactly one file, `plex_blip_watchdog.service.j2`. There is no
   timer. The 2026-08-04 plan *proposed* one
   (`.agents/planning/2026-08-04-debug-plex-blip/implementation/plan.md:116`) and it was never
   built; this runbook describes it as existing and attributes it to Plex itself. Charged to 3c,
   which must census the whole 27-line file rather than the one sentence `plan.md` names.

9. **The sentence `plan.md` quotes is a paraphrase.** `:7` actually reads *"To prevent
   transaction lock stalls (`TX_STALL`) **and ensure high concurrency for reads** …"* — a compound
   clause. 3c writes its shape test against the file's bytes, not against `plan.md`'s quotation.

10. **The audit has no `-readonly`.** `tasks/main.yml:346-349` opens the library database
    read-write to ask it a question about lock contention. `plan.md` Step 8 makes *"every
    `sqlite3` invocation carries `-readonly`"* a shape test; 3b rewrites this exact invocation, so
    it is charged there — the cheapest time to fix a guard is inside the edit that makes it
    reachable.

### No filed row is armed by Step 3, re-derived by census rather than quoted

`plan.md` Step 11's materialization rule promotes a filed row only when a numbered step makes its
defect **reachable**. Checked at my own hands, not from the filer's prose:

## Step 1 → Step 2 wave cut — `queue.advance` (Planner, 2026-08-08, DEC-226 consumed)

**Step 2's wave is cut and 2a is routed.** The current step moves for the first time in this
objective. Three things were decided here, and the third had been deferred twice.

### 1. The operator gate is a task, not a park

Step 1's Demo became `task-1786133422-3791` — P1, operator-only, with the deploy content
enumerated by commit and the Finalizer's two-leg measurement quoted so the operator can see
the artifact is producible before spending a deploy on it. It carries a batching note: Step 3's
Demo is *also* a `just play`, so one deploy can eventually carry both.

The gate does **not** block Step 2, and the reason is a property of Step 2 rather than a
preference: `check_trigger` and `analyze_plex_blips.py` are string parsing over a corpus
already on disk. Nothing in the wave reads a deployed artifact. Emitting `tasks.ready` here is
therefore not the Builder-closes-the-gate trap — the Builder is handed real, unblocked,
non-operator work, and the gate sits beside it untouched.

This is the lesson from two prior objectives in this repo, applied rather than re-derived: on
plex-monitoring, iterations 88–90 each met an operator gate and parked with an empty
`tasks.ready` until the operator restarted the runner and iteration 91 took a filed row that
closed a real hole. Parking is correct when the ready set is *entirely* operator-gated. It is
wrong when eight numbered steps are untouched.

### 2. The wave's premises were measured, not inherited

Six findings, all in *Active Wave* above. The load-bearing one is the **basename rule**: the
raw log carries a 90-character CI build path where `plan.md`'s acceptance names
`StatisticsManager.cpp:288`, so a Builder capturing the parenthesised group verbatim writes
correct-looking code that fails the criterion. That is the same class as the two false
contracts caught at the 1b and 1c cuts (`playbooks/plex.yml`, which never existed; a task name
that did not exist), and it was found the same way — by running the greps instead of reading
the plan.

Two more worth carrying: the gate stays at **39/39** because Step 2 extends already-collected
files (an unchanged count is a pass here, the *inverse* of 1c's reading), and
`test_analyze_plex_blips.py:149`/`:218` are **vacuously green** against a `/tmp` path that does
not exist.

### 3. The homeless P2 rows now have a home — `plan.md` Step 11

This question was deferred at the 1b cut ("*at that moment the wave is exhausted and 5963 will
be the only row left standing*") and again at the 1c cut ("*should be answered against Step 2's
bar*"). Answering it a third time by deferring would be the churn the record keeps warning
about, so it is answered.

**Priced against Step 2's bar first, by census at my own hands rather than from the filers'
prose.** `_run_cmd` has exactly four call sites at `7021470` — `:405`, `:406`, `:417`, `:418`
— and none is inside a loop; the module's only loops are at `:173`/`:195` inside `_fd_count`.
`_dry_run_fallback` is called from `:409`/`:412`/`:420`, all inside `if self.dry_run:`. Step 2
adds no `_run_cmd` call and no subprocess at all. So **neither row is armed by Step 2** and
neither is routed. That much is the same answer as the last two cuts.

**What changes is that "not routed" is no longer the same as "nowhere".** `plan.md` gains
**Step 11 — Guard-strength backlog**, whose members are `task-1786124338-5963`,
`task-1786128505-658f` and the newly filed `task-1786133552-65f2`, with an explicit
materialization rule: Step 11's wave exists when Steps 2–10 close, **or** a row is promoted
early into whatever numbered step arms it — because the cheapest moment to fix a guard is
inside the edit that makes it reachable. Each row's description already carries its own
promotion trigger.

No wave was materialized for Step 11 and none may be until that rule fires; naming a step is
not the same as creating its work. The three rows were **not** re-`ensure`d to fit the new
namespace either — `ensure` rewrites the record, all three carry long measured descriptions,
and only a false contract licenses a rewrite. Membership lives in `plan.md`, which is where
strategy belongs.

### One thing repaired that was nobody's row

`scripts/test_plex_watchdog_deps_shape.py` was still **uncommitted** at this activation — the
whole of 1c round 3 plus the Finalizer's two in-hat docstring repairs, reviewed at DEC-225,
finalized, and closed with the delivery sitting in the working tree. Every other round of this
loop committed. Left as-is it would hand the Step 2 Builder a polluted `git diff --numstat`
baseline and a `git checkout` restore that destroys 85 lines of reviewed guard — the exact
hazard the 1b Builder hit and had to work around by restoring from bytes.

Committed as `7021470` after re-running the standalone test at the committed bytes (**4/4
PASS**). No file content changed; this is bookkeeping for a closed row, not implementation, and
it is recorded here rather than done silently.

**Active task:** `code-assist:plex-blip-manual-triage:step-01:snapshot-structured-shape`.

`scripts/test_plex_blip_watchdog.py`. `git diff --numstat`: **35 7** on the module,
**187 0** on the test file — zero deleted test lines, so acceptance 3 holds at its
strongest reading.

### The casualty was live, and it went red before it went green

Five of the six new rows in `TestSnapshotStructuredShape` were **RED at `d700c9b`**
(`logs/step1b-red.txt`, 5 failures + 4 errors) — this is not a reconstruction, it is the
delivered tree failing:

- `--dry-run stopped simulating: lock_holders.fuser has no fallback text` /
  `... lock_holders.lsof ...` — `output` was `None`, exactly `context.md` §3's prediction.
- `'[dry-run] lsof simulated check OK' not found in ''` — the second arm, a probe that ran
  and said nothing.
- `KeyError: 'pidstat'` on `process_traces` (four sites) — the placement move.

`logs/step1b-green.txt`: **6/6 OK**. Full suite **24/24** (was 18). `just test`
**GATE PASS 38/38**.

### Acceptance 2 — the three sites, named, with their new test

All three now go through one helper, `_dry_run_fallback` at `:145-163` (predicate at `:161`), so the predicate
lives in **one** place. Three copies of a condition that were all wrong together is what
produced this defect; the guards below hold each site individually regardless.

| Was (string test on `_run_cmd`'s dict) | Is now |
|---|---|
| `:388 if not fuser_out or "probe error" in fuser_out or fuser_out == "":` | `:409 fuser_out = _dry_run_fallback(fuser_out, "[dry-run] fuser simulated check OK for …")` |
| `:390 if not lsof_out or "probe error" in lsof_out or lsof_out == "":` | `:412 lsof_out = _dry_run_fallback(lsof_out, "[dry-run] lsof simulated check OK for …")` |
| `:396 if self.dry_run and ("probe error" in pidstat_out or not pidstat_out):` | `:420 pidstat_out = _dry_run_fallback(pidstat_out, "[dry-run] pidstat diagnostic snapshot OK")` |

The predicate is `result.get("status") != "ok" or not result.get("output")` — the status
field, not a substring of the output. The second clause is deliberate and is **not** a
leftover string test: it is the `fuser_out == ""` arm the old code had, and real
`fuser`/`lsof` exit 1 with empty output when nothing holds the file. See DEC-216.

### Acceptance 4 — the real CLI, in the production no-`psmisc` case

`logs/step1b-cli-dryrun.txt`: the actual script, live inotify path, `--dry-run
--max-triggers 1`, `PATH` set to a stub dir holding **only** a real `ps` so
`fuser`/`lsof`/`pidstat` are genuinely absent (the stub dir **alone** — mem-1786124365-add6:
prepending is not enough). One JSONL record, round-trips, eight top-level keys:

```

lock_holders.lsof     status=missing elapsed_ms=0.18  output='[dry-run] lsof simulated check OK for …'
```

This is the run the Finalizer made at `d700c9b` and reported as **zero of three fallbacks

`test_top_level_keys_are_exactly_the_eight_already_on_disk` is the **no-removal** guard; it

**M3 was mis-aimed on the first run and the harness caught it rather than me.** Mutating

the helper is only reachable from inside `if self.dry_run:`. The leak that guard exists for

worth as much as one it fails.

## Round 2 — DEC-217 rejection consumed (Builder, task-1786120230-df98)

**The verdict was "the code is correct, a guard is missing", and it is.** No source change:
`plex_blip_watchdog.py` is byte-identical at `d4b4945c…b590a` before and after, verified by
both harnesses. The whole fix is ONE test row.

### The hole

`_dry_run_fallback`'s predicate (`:161`) has two halves. The suite pinned that the fallback
**fires** — `missing`, and `ok`-but-empty — and that it never fires in a **live** capture.
Nothing pinned the discriminating half: that it does **not** fire when a probe ran and
*spoke*. So H1 (`if True:`, substitute unconditionally) stayed GREEN across all 24 rows
while `--dry-run` overwrote real `fuser`/`lsof` output with a simulated line still carrying
`status="ok"`, a real `elapsed_ms` and `error=None` — a fabrication wearing every mark of a
probe that ran, which is this row's own failure class inverted.

### The row

`test_a_probe_that_ran_and_spoke_keeps_its_own_output_under_dry_run`, plus the
`_install_speaking_stub` helper. `dry_run=True`, `PATH` = the stub dir **alone**, stubs that
exit 0 with **distinct** non-empty text per probe (so a fuser/lsof cross-wiring fails too,
which "no `[dry-run]` anywhere" alone would miss). Asserts each of `:409`/`:412`/`:420` keeps
its own output and that `[dry-run]` appears nowhere in the probe's record.

Stubs use **shell builtins only** — `PATH` as the stub dir alone strips `coreutils` from the
stub's *own* lookup, so a `cat <<EOF` stub dies with `cat: not found` and the harness
measures its own bug (mem-1786126267-0e0d).

### Verification — `logs/builder-df98-r2-speaking-arm.py`

| leg | result |
|---|---|
| new row ALONE, delivered tree | **GREEN** (3 subtests) |
| new row ALONE under H1 | **RED**, 3 failed |
| suite **without** the row under H1 | **GREEN** 24/24 — the Critic's finding, reproduced |
| H4 `if False:` / H5 swap (controls) | **RED** 6 / **RED** 2 — the suite kept its teeth |
| H3 drop the status clause | GREEN and **INERT**, not chased per DEC-217 |

The third line is the one that matters: it shows *this row* is what turns H1 red, not some
unrelated collateral failure.

**The Critic's own harness, unmodified, now returns `no hole; H1 was caught`** — and its
LEG A still passes on the delivered tree (real CLI, live inotify, `--dry-run`, speaking
probes, one written JSONL record with all three sites keeping real output). Preserved at
`logs/builder-df98-r2-critic-harness-post-fix.txt`; note the re-run overwrote the Critic's
original `logs/critic-df98-predicate-holes.txt` in place.

### Harness bug I hit, and the lesson (mem-1786126777-2565)

My first attribution attempt scraped `FAILED`-prefixed lines from pytest's summary and found
**none** while `rc != 0` — with `pytest-subtests` a subtest failure is not reported on a
`FAILED` line. It read as "went red but didn't name my row" when the row was in fact killing
H1. Attribute *positively* — row-alone RED plus suite-without-row GREEN — never by parsing
the summary.

### Acceptance

1. RED/GREEN both pasted above. 2. Three sites named, one helper, predicate at `:161`.
3. **Additive only: `git diff --numstat` = `56  0`, zero lines deleted or modified.**
4. Real CLI `--dry-run` JSONL record — Critic LEG A, re-run green.
5. `just test` **GATE PASS 38/38**, exit 0. Suite 25 rows / 16 subtests.

Fix part (2), amending mem-1786125615-f73d, was already carried by the Critic's
mem-1786126284-fe3e; runtime memories are append-only (no `update` subcommand), so the
resolution and the new attribution lesson are recorded in mem-1786126777-2565.

| case | result |
|---|---|
| LIVE-MISSING (only a real `ps` — the production no-psmisc reality) | 8/8 keys, `dry_run:false`, **no `[dry-run]`/`simulated` text anywhere in the record**, all three probes `missing` with real `error` strings and real `elapsed_ms` (0.327 / 0.186 / 0.113), `ps_aux_t` `ok` with real output |
| LIVE-SLOW (`lsof` blocks past 2.0 s) | `{status:"timeout", elapsed_ms:2002.50, error:"probe timeout exceeded (2.0s)"}` — **the number is the timeout, not the 30 s the command wanted, and not zero** |

The second row is plan.md:45-47's "the regression that matters most", scored on the live path
for the first time. It is also the input Step 4 exports as
`plex_watchdog_probe_duration_seconds`, so it mattered to check it outside `--dry-run`.
