## Step 1 wave cut — `queue.advance` after 1b closed (Planner, 2026-08-08, DEC-220)

Step 1 stays the current step and **no Step 2 wave was created**. The wave is not exhausted:
1a and 1b are closed, **1c (`task-1786120230-f02c`) is open, P1, unblocked** — so the queue
contract's rule 3 applies (ready work exists for the current step; route the next row in the
same wave) and rule 5 does not. There was no pricing question between rows: 1c is the only
Step 1 row left standing.

**Routed: 1c (`task-1786120230-f02c`).** It is also the row that decides the step. Step 1's
Demo is *real `fuser` output for the first time since deployment*; `fuser` ships in `psmisc`;
`psmisc` is in no apt name list in the repo. Re-verified at `686e3b8` rather than carried:
the only `psmisc` string under `ansible/` is a docstring at `plex_blip_watchdog.py:150`.

### The row's record was re-`ensure`d — acceptance 3 was a false contract

Per the standing rule, every pointer and harness the routed row's criteria cite was executed
or grepped before routing. Acceptance 3 said:

> `ansible-playbook --syntax-check playbooks/plex.yml` passes.

**There is no `playbooks/` directory in this repo and there never has been.**
`git log --all -- playbooks/ ansible/playbooks/` returns nothing. The criterion has been
unsatisfiable since the row was written at `5c9a830`, and the DEC-215 correction pass —
which repaired criteria 1 and 2's pointers — did not check it. Measured at `686e3b8`:

| invocation | result |
|---|---|
| `ansible-playbook --syntax-check playbooks/plex.yml` | **exit 1** — `[ERROR]: the playbook: playbooks/plex.yml could not be found` |
| `cd ansible && ANSIBLE_VAULT_PASSWORD=CHANGEME mise exec -- ansible-playbook --syntax-check site.yml` | **exit 0** — `playbook: site.yml` |

The failure mode is the expensive kind: a Builder who has just edited `tasks/main.yml` runs
the cited command, sees `[ERROR]` and exit 1, and reads it as *my change broke the playbook*.
That is a burned round, which is exactly what the false-contract rule exists to prevent.

The repo has **one** playbook, `ansible/site.yml`, and its third play is
`- name: Plex media server with Intel QSV hardware transcoding / hosts: plex / roles: - plex`,
so `site.yml` **is** the syntax check for this role. `scripts/run_gate.py:68` already runs
exactly that step — which also means acceptance 3 is a **subset of acceptance 4**, not a
second harness. Repaired in place; the stale spelling survives only inside the dated
correction block, and the record was read back out of `tasks.jsonl` to verify the `ensure`
side effect (`open`, P1, `blocked_by []`, 4405 → 7496 chars, exactly two correction blocks,
zero occurrences of the stale spelling outside them).

**One passenger repaired in the same `ensure`:** the row cited the `fuser` probe at
`plex_blip_watchdog.py:331`, the `5c9a830` spelling. Rounds 1a and 1b moved it — at `686e3b8`
it is `:405`. This is the DEC-215 shape (*a pointer into the tree that has moved*), so it was
corrected in place rather than left to be rediscovered, even though on its own it gates no
acceptance criterion and would not have licensed an `ensure`.

*Measured before "correcting", per the standing warning that a candidate fix can itself be
the false claim:* both invocations above were run at my own hands before the record was
touched, and PyYAML 6.0.3 was re-confirmed importable under `.venv/bin/python` — so the row's
"parse as YAML" criterion is still available and still a choice, not a constraint.

### Gate arithmetic, written down so a changed count is not read as a regression

`run_gate.py:53` globs `scripts/test_*.py`, so a **new shape test joins the gate
automatically**. At `686e3b8`: 33 shape tests + 3 tofu + 2 ansible = the **38/38** every
round of 1a and 1b reported. This row's test makes it **39/39**. A green gate that still
says 38 means the new file was never picked up — a vacuous pass wearing a green badge.

### The two homeless P2 rows were re-priced, and both are still outranked

`task-1786124338-5963` (looped `_run_cmd` call site under-counted by the latency bound) and
`task-1786128505-658f` (dry-run fallback pins `elapsed_ms`/`error` by presence, so a
fabricated value walks through) are both ready, both P2, and both belong to **no numbered
step**. Neither is routed and neither is given a blocker.

- **Nothing arms either.** 5963 re-verified at `686e3b8` by census, not by prose: `_run_cmd`
  has four call sites (`:405`, `:406`, `:417`, `:418`) and **none is inside a loop**, and no
  step in the ten-step plan adds one. 658f's premise was re-verified by the Finalizer at this
  same sha — `_dry_run_fallback`, "dry-run fallback" and "simulated" are **no hits at all**
  across `plan.md` and `detailed-design.md` — and the helper is reachable only under
  `if self.dry_run:`, so no production capture can carry a fabricated field.
- **1c outranks both on the step's own bar.** 1c is P1 work the Step 1 Demo cannot happen
  without; these two are P2 guard strength on a path production never takes.
- **Left visibly ready on purpose,** and *not* re-blocked to tidy the set: `ensure` rewrites
  the record, so re-blocking risks clobbering long measured descriptions, and routing is
  decided by the `tasks.ready` payload rather than by the ready list.

This does **not** pre-commit the Planner who cuts Step 2. When 1c closes, Step 1's wave is
exhausted and 5963/658f are the only rows left standing — at that moment the plan still has
Steps 2–10 unchecked, so the homeless-row question is a genuinely different pricing question
from this one and should be answered against Step 2's bar, not against this note.

`psmisc` added to the **"Install Plex watchdog and diagnostic dependencies"** apt task
(`ansible/roles/plex/tasks/main.yml:243`, between `procps` and `sqlite3`), plus a new shape

package plus a comment naming `fuser` and saying why not `plex_media_packages`) and one new
test file. Nothing else in `ansible/` was touched.

**Acceptance 1 — RED before, GREEN after, both runs pasted.**

    logs/builder-f02c-red.txt    FAIL 2/4  (psmisc=False, homes=[])           exit 1
    logs/builder-f02c-green.txt  PASS 4/4                                     exit 0

The RED is for the right reason and not a lookup failure: the anti-vacuity row is **OK** in
the same run (`found=True packages=['sysstat','lsof','procps','sqlite3']`), so the test
located the real task and then found `psmisc` missing from it.

**Acceptance 2 — the negative row is present, and its discrimination is measured, not
argued.** It reads `plex_media_packages` from `defaults/main.yml` (the list literal), never
from `tasks/main.yml`, which carries only `{{ plex_media_packages }}` and cannot answer the
question. Because that row is GREEN on *both* sides of this commit, RED→GREEN says nothing
about it — so the wrong-placement worlds were built and scored
(`logs/builder-f02c-placement-mutations.py`, mutating the **ansible tree** and never the
test, byte-restored with a sha256 assert; **7/7 behaved as specified**):

    C0 delivered tree                                    -> 4/4 GREEN
    W1 psmisc MOVED to plex_media_packages               -> RED: positive + census + negative
    W2 psmisc in BOTH homes                              -> RED: negative row ALONE
    W3 psmisc moved to the plexmediaserver apt task      -> RED: positive + census
    W4 watchdog-deps task RENAMED (vacuous lookup)       -> RED: anti-vacuity row
    W5 list REPLACED by [psmisc] (drops lsof/procps)     -> RED: positive row
    W6 plex_media_packages DELETED from defaults         -> RED: negative row

W2 is the row's whole justification in one line: belt-and-braces placement in both homes is
invisible to the positive row and to the census, and only the negative row sees it. W6 is the
negative row's own anti-vacuity — a deleted list would make "psmisc not in it" trivially true,
so the row also asserts the list is real *and* still consumed by an apt task through the
template.

**Beyond the row's stated negative:** the role has **four** apt name lists, and
`plex_media_packages` is only one of three wrong homes. `test_psmisc_has_exactly_one_apt_home_in_the_role`
censuses them all, which is what catches W3 — a placement the row's stated negative misses.

**Acceptance 3 —** `(cd ansible && ANSIBLE_VAULT_PASSWORD=CHANGEME mise exec -- \
ansible-playbook --syntax-check site.yml)` → **exit 0**, `playbook: site.yml`. The
`[WARNING]: Found both group and host with same name: plex` is pre-existing inventory naming
and untouched by an 8-line apt diff. Per the row's 2026-08-08 correction this is a subset of
acceptance 4, not a second harness.

**Acceptance 4 —** `just test` → **GATE PASS: 39/39, exit 0** (`logs/builder-f02c-gate.txt`).
This is the Planner's predicted arithmetic exactly, 38 → 39, and the new file was **actually
collected** — line 593 shows `=== shape: scripts/test_plex_watchdog_deps_shape.py ===` running
under `.venv/bin/python` and returning exit 0. A green gate still reading 38 would have been
the vacuous pass the Planner warned about. `ansible-lint --offline --profile production` is
green, so the added comment does not upset the production profile.

### Two premises I measured instead of inheriting

1. **`fuser` really does ship in `psmisc`** — the whole row rests on it and every prior turn
   asserted it. `rpm -qf $(command -v fuser)` → `psmisc-23.7-7.fc44.x86_64`. Same upstream
   package on Debian. The probe it unblocks is still `plex_blip_watchdog.py:405`, and that
   module's own docstring at `:99` calls the symptom "34/34 `fuser` failures were one missing
   package" — which is this change.
2. **PyYAML under the gate interpreter.** `test_plex_state_ownership_shape.py:249-252` says
   the gate pythons lack PyYAML and that YAML helpers are reachable only after its re-exec.
   That is stale for `.venv/bin/python`: `just test` runs `python scripts/run_gate.py`, both
   `python` and `sys.executable` resolve to `.venv/bin/python`, and it carries **PyYAML
   6.0.3**. Measured before writing a line of the test, and then confirmed end-to-end by the
   file passing *inside* the gate. The new test therefore imports `yaml` at module level with
   **no try/except skip**, deliberately: the repo's `OK (skip): not installed` idiom would
   turn a missing parser into a green badge over an unrun check.

**Why YAML and not the stdlib+regex convention:** containment is the entire point of this row
— `psmisc` installs the binary from any of the four lists, so a `"psmisc" in main.yml` grep is
green for every placement. Parsing also dodges a false positive a regex would take: `sqlite3`
appears again at `:339` inside a `command: argv:` list, so "name appears in the file" is not
even a reliable proxy for "package is installed".

**Not in this row, unchanged:** the deploy/demo. Step 1's Demo (real `fuser` output in a JSONL
snapshot) needs 1a+1b+1c all landed and is the Step 1 close, not this row. The two homeless P2
rows (`5963`, `658f`) were not touched.

## Step 1c round 2 — census blindness repaired (Builder, DEC-221 rejection consumed, DEC-222)

**Active task:** `task-1786120230-f02c`, key
`code-assist:plex-blip-manual-triage:step-01:psmisc-dependency`. Round 1 was REJECTED at
`874a313` on the passenger, not on the substance: all four stated acceptance criteria held
and the Critic re-ran every one. The charge was that the census row's docstring claimed a
closure — "including any apt task added after this file was written" — that `_apt_tasks()`
did not have. **No ansible source changed this round;** the only edited file is
`scripts/test_plex_watchdog_deps_shape.py`, and `git status` over `ansible/` is clean.

### The three repairs, all inside the census walk

| # | Hole | Repair |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | `args.get("name")` reads one of the apt module's **three** package-parameter spellings | `args.get("name") or args.get("pkg") or args.get("package")` (`APT_NAME_KEYS`) |
| 2 | the walk is over the top-level task list, so a `block:`-nested apt task is invisible | `_iter_tasks()` recursively flattens `block`/`rescue`/`always` (`BLOCK_KEYS`) |
| 3 | the row named "is **installed** by" stayed green under `state: absent` | positive row reads `state`, against apt's closed choice list (`APT_INSTALLING_STATES`) |

Repairs 1 and 2 were charged; 3 was passed forward as a recommendation and taken — see
DEC-222 for why `when:` (the other half of that recommendation) was declined instead.

### Verification commands and results

    .venv/bin/python logs/builder-f02c-r2-census-blindness.py   # RED  -> exit 1, 4 MISSED
    .venv/bin/python logs/builder-f02c-r2-census-blindness.py   # GREEN-> exit 0, 6/6 CAUGHT
    .venv/bin/python scripts/test_plex_watchdog_deps_shape.py   # PASS: 4/4, exit 0
    .venv/bin/python logs/builder-f02c-placement-mutations.py   # 7/7, round-1 battery intact
    just test                                                   # GATE PASS: 39/39 steps, exit 0

`logs/builder-f02c-r2-census-blindness.py` mutates the **ansible tree** and never the test,
sha256-asserts a byte restore per path, and holds the control green on both sides of every
run. It was executed unchanged before and after the test-file edit, so the difference between

    B1  second psmisc home inside block:     MISSED (all four rows green)  ->  CAUGHT (one_home)
    B2  second psmisc home via pkg:          MISSED                        ->  CAUGHT (one_home)
    B3  second psmisc home via package:      MISSED                        ->  CAUGHT (one_home)
    A1  state: absent on the watchdog task   MISSED                        ->  CAUGHT (positive)
    W1  psmisc removed        (control)      CAUGHT                        ->  CAUGHT
    W3  psmisc on plexmediaserver (control)  CAUGHT                        ->  CAUGHT

**B3 is not in the rejection.** DEC-221 named all three spellings but built only `pkg:`.
`package:` is the *canonical* parameter —
`package=dict(type='list', elements='str', aliases=['pkg', 'name'])` at ansible-core 2.21.1
`modules/apt.py:1274`, read here rather than carried from the review — and it missed
identically, which is why the repair is the whole alias set and not the one alias.

## 2026-08-08 — Step 1c round 3 (Builder), DEC-223 rejection consumed

**Task:** `task-1786120230-f02c` (`code-assist:plex-blip-manual-triage:step-01:psmisc-dependency`)

### The charge and the repair

DEC-223 charged ONE sentence: `:189` said "No task in this role carries one today" about
`when:`, and `tasks/main.yml:145` (multi-line `when: >-` on the PMS-stop task) and `:191`
(`when: plex_chown_needed`) both carry one. Verified at my own hands. The undecidability half
of the decline is true; the factual half was false, and it is the half that turned
undecidability into a decision.

Repaired as the review specified — key PRESENCE, never value. A `when:` VALUE is arbitrary
Jinja with no closed set; the KEY is there or it is not, as complete as apt's `state:` choice
list, and it evaluates no Jinja.

**Built at the census's own radius, not just the task's.** A `when:` on an ENCLOSING block
gates every task inside it, and the one precedent this file cites to arm `BLOCK_KEYS` —
`docker_host/tasks/main.yml:357` — is itself `when: docker_host_acme_reset | bool` sitting ON
that block. Reading only the task's own key would be the charged defect at a smaller radius.
`_iter_tasks` now carries gates down the recursion; `_apt_tasks` reports them as a 4th field.

`logs/builder-f02c-r3-when-worlds.py` mutates the ANSIBLE tree and never the test,
sha256-asserts the byte restore, control green both sides, run unmodified before and after the
test-file edit — **4 MISSED -> 0 MISSED, 5/5 caught**, every kill on the `positive` row:

|---|---|---|

| C4 deps task inside a `when: <jinja>` block | MISSED, 4 rows green | CAUGHT |
| W1 psmisc removed (control) | CAUGHT | CAUGHT |

the C3 tree. Logs `logs/builder-f02c-r3-red.txt` / `-green.txt`.

**No prior guard regressed** (`logs/builder-f02c-r3-prior-batteries.txt`): round 1 placement
battery **7/7**, round 2 census-blindness **6/6, 0 MISSED**, and the Critic's own

DEC-225 passed the round while naming both. Neither is load-bearing on a guard.

`logs/finalizer-f02c-psmisc-is-the-difference.py` drives the actual watchdog twice through

| leg | `lock_holders.fuser` |
|---|---|

corroborated off the OS package database rather than `plan.md`'s word: `rpm -qf $(command -v
fuser)` → `psmisc-23.7-7.fc44`.

**The result that looked wrong was chased, not banked.** The `ok` leg returned **empty
output**, and `fuser -v` writes its holder table to **stderr** — which would mean the Step 1
demo yields `status="ok"` and nothing to read. It does not: `_run_cmd:110` sets
`stderr=subprocess.STDOUT`, proven by execution — with a live process holding the db file the
probe returns the real `USER PID ACCESS COMMAND` table plus the holder pid; with nothing
holding it, empty. **Step 1's demo artifact is genuinely producible once deployed.**

**Demo** — an operator `just play` — is what closes it. Steps 2–10 are untouched, so this is
`queue.advance` on either ground.

All six acceptance criteria re-derived at this turn rather than read off DEC-228, plus one
measurement neither the Builder nor the Critic made. Nothing was reopened.

### Re-derived, at my own hands

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `just test` (`.venv/bin/python scripts/run_gate.py`) | **GATE PASS 39/39 exit 0** — `logs/finalizer-2a-gate.txt`, `test_plex_blip_watchdog.py` visibly collected at `:568-569` |
| Watchdog suite | **37/37 OK** |
| Analyzer suite | **16/16 OK**, untouched (the two `SKIP:` lines are `task-1786133552-65f2`, not this row) |
| AC2 06:37:33.273 holder | `TX_HELD`, `delay_ms 540.0`, `hold_site "StatisticsManager.cpp:288"` ✓, fixture byte-identical to the corpus |
| AC3 `[Req#8ec76]` `:459` | `TX_HELD`, `hold_site "MetadataItemSetting.cpp:459"` ✓ |
| AC4 regression waiter | still `TX_STALL`, `delay_ms 110.0` ✓ |
| AC5 `(13 live)` | both corpus lines (06:40:46.067 `Completed:`, 06:40:46.096 `Request:`) → `13` ✓ |
| Scope fence | `capture_snapshot` / `_run_cmd` / `_dry_run_fallback` **AST-segment byte-identical** to `7021470`; 4 `_run_cmd` call sites, none looped — P2 rows `5963` and `658f` stay unarmed |
| Step 1's carry-forward constraint | the 448,202 bytes / 34 records already on disk are **key-homogeneous at 8/8**, and the delivered record's key set is exactly that set |

### What nobody had measured: the DEBOUNCE differential

Every measurement in this thread has been **per line** — the Builder's 42/42 census, the
Critic's 703,425-line classification differential. Per-line classification is not what the
watchdog writes to disk. The run loop composes classification with a **30 s debounce**, so a
new trigger inserted into the stream can move a capture earlier *and evict a later one*.
Neither is visible in a per-line diff.

`logs/finalizer-a3cd-debounce-differential.py` → `logs/finalizer-a3cd-debounce.txt` replays
both modules (`7021470` and HEAD) over the real 2026-08-07 log, driving
`should_trigger`/`record_trigger` with **the log's own timestamps**. Wall clock is the wrong
instrument for a replay — the file is consumed in milliseconds, so a wall-clock debounce fires
once for the whole day; log time is what production sees, because in production the lines
arrive as they are written.

- **14 captures → 14 captures.** The capture *count* is unchanged.
- **The first capture does not move**: `06:37:25.337 SLOW_QUERY` on both. So `plan.md:81-83`'s
  binding framing — *"do not justify this as making the watchdog fire earlier; it does not"* —
  is **true, and measured for the first time** rather than asserted.
- **Exactly one capture changes.** At `06:39:06` the old module captured the waiter
  (`TX_STALL`, `.480`); the new one captures the holder (`TX_HELD`, `.340`,
  `MetadataItemSetting.cpp:459`, 1020 ms) **140 ms earlier, evicting it**. That is Step 2's
  objective landing on the artifact: the snapshot now names the code site instead of the
  ripple. It is an improvement, but it *is* a change to what gets written, and Step 4 exports
  `plex_watchdog_events_total{event_type=…}` from these captures — so it belongs in the record
  rather than in a commit message that only claims `capture_snapshot`'s *shape* is unchanged.
- **6 of 14** captures now carry a `live_connections` count.

**A distinction worth keeping straight before 2c's Demo.** The 06:37:33.273
`StatisticsManager.cpp:288` holder — the line 2c's Demo must name — is **never captured by the
watchdog**: the debounce window opened at 06:37:25.337 and closes at 06:37:55. The *analyzer*
sees all 42 holder lines; the *watchdog* sees one. 2c's Demo is an analyzer run and is
unaffected, but "the first holder at 06:37:33" is an analyzer claim, not a snapshot claim.

### Real CLI on the production path, three legs — 20/20

`logs/finalizer-a3cd-real-cli.py` → `logs/finalizer-a3cd-real-cli.txt`. Live inotify, real
subprocess, **no `--dry-run`** — every prior real leg in this thread drove the dry-run path.

- **LEG A** the corpus pair in corpus order → one record, `TX_HELD`, `delay_ms 1020.0`,
  `dry_run false`, `trigger_line` byte-identical to the corpus holder line. The eviction the
  differential predicted, confirmed on the artifact.
- **LEG B — ADVERSARIAL, order reversed.** The debounce is order-sensitive and blind to event
  type, so waiter-first → `TX_STALL` and the holder is **debounced away**. Adding `TX_HELD`
  does not *guarantee* holder attribution; the first line wins. This is the honest bound on
  what Step 2 buys and no leg had driven it.
- **LEG C — ADVERSARIAL, degenerate site `()`.** Does not trigger, and therefore cannot evict
  a good waiter capture.

### The a6e1 finding, re-verified at the artifact rather than trusted

LEG A's record carries **exactly eight** top-level keys — `hold_site` and `live_connections`
are **absent**. The site is recoverable only by re-parsing `trigger_line`, where it is still
the full 90-character CI path, un-basenamed. So the one capture Step 2a actually changes is
the one whose defining new field never reaches the artifact. `task-1786134818-a6e1` (2d) is
real, it is P1, and it is now the strongest-evidenced row in the wave.

### Decision: `queue.advance`, not `LOOP_COMPLETE`

Three independent reasons, any one sufficient: Step 2 has **2b, 2c and 2d open**; Step 1's
**Demo has never been exercised** (`task-1786133422-3791`, operator-only); and **Steps 3–10
are untouched** with Step 11 carrying three filed members. A passed row is not a passed step.

## Step 2 intra-wave routing — `queue.advance` after 2a closed (Planner, 2026-08-08, DEC-228 consumed)

**Step 2 stays the current step; its wave is not exhausted.** 2a is closed at `9f86452`; 2b,
2c and 2d are open, all P1. No next-step wave was created and none may be — 2c carries Step 2's
Demo and Step 2's Integration line is 2d's.

**Routed: 2d (`task-1786134818-a6e1`).** Two rows were ready (2b and 2d) and priority does not
separate them. Three things do, in order of weight:

1. **2d is the only row that can be silently skipped.** 2b unblocks 2c and 2c is the Demo, so
   both are structurally load-bearing — a wave that forgets them does not close. 2d is
   independent, keyless, and filed *after* the wave was cut against a `plan.md` line
   (`plan.md:95`, Step 2's **Integration**) whose owner was nobody. The row at risk of being
   dropped goes first; the rows the queue itself protects can wait.
2. **It rides on the freshest contract.** 2d reads `trigger.get("hold_site")` /
   `trigger.get("live_connections")` out of the dict 2a landed one commit ago, with 2a's
   absent-not-None convention (DEC-227) still the newest fact in the record. That is the same
   argument that put 2b behind 2a: the vocabulary is a published contract and the cheapest time
   to consume it is before anything else has re-spelled it.
3. **It is the strongest-evidenced row in the wave**, and not by its own filer's prose — the
   Finalizer drove a real holder line through the production path (real CLI, no `--dry-run`,
   20/20) and the written record carried **exactly the eight** top-level keys with `hold_site`
   and `live_connections` absent, the site recoverable only by re-parsing `trigger_line` as the
   un-basenamed 90-character CI path. The one capture Step 2a changes is the one missing its
   defining field.

### 2c was re-`ensure`d, and the license is a false contract — not a re-scope

`task-1786133527-ce9e` kept its id, key, title, priority and all five acceptance criteria; a
dated correction block was appended (2974 → 5658 chars) and `--blocked-by` grew from `2b` to
`2b, 2d`. The standing rule is that only a false contract licenses rewriting a record. One
sentence in 2c had become false: **"THIS ROW CARRIES STEP 2'S DEMO. Step 2 closes on it."**
That was true of the three-row wave it was written against. 2d was filed afterwards, so a
Finalizer reading 2c today closes Step 2 with `plan.md:95` unmet and nothing red — the same
class as `playbooks/plex.yml` and the non-existent task name caught at the 1b and 1c cuts. The
false sentence is quoted in the correction rather than deleted, per the flip-the-polarity rule.

The block carries two more things, both measured here rather than carried:

- **The Demo's trap, handed over by the Finalizer and confirmed.** "the first holder at
  06:37:33" is an **analyzer** claim and is true for the analyzer. It is not true of the
  watchdog: the debounce window opened at 06:37:25.337 on a `SLOW_QUERY`, so the watchdog never
  captures that line. Analyzer sees 42 holder lines; watchdog captures 1. Without this in the
  row, a Builder chases a JSONL capture that correctly does not exist.
- **The corpus census, at this Planner's own hands** (`/tmp/plex-logs/2026-08-07/`): 42
  `Held transaction` lines — **27× `MetadataItemSetting.cpp:459`, 13× `MetadataItemSetting.cpp:409`,
  2× `StatisticsManager.cpp:288`**; first holder at `:3284`, 06:37:33.273, `StatisticsManager.cpp:288`,
  `0.540000 seconds`. Acceptance 5's "first holder at 06:37:33" is therefore **confirmed**, not
  carried from `plan.md`. The census also says something `plan.md`'s Demo sentence does not:
  `:288` is 2 of 42 and the **dominant** site is `:459` at 27. A report that names the first
  holder and hides the loudest one satisfies the sentence and misses the corpus, so that is now
  in the row too.

### The wave's premises, re-derived at this activation rather than read off DEC-228

- Watchdog suite **37/37 OK**; analyzer suite **16/16 OK** with the two known `SKIP:` lines
  (`task-1786133552-65f2`, Step 11) — the vacuous-green pair is unchanged and still not this

- 2b's acceptance 3 is **true and not a false contract**: `:3294` is
  `[Req#8ec76] … MetadataItemSetting.cpp:459): 0.310000 seconds`, verbatim. Checked because

### The absent case is `None`, not a missing key — DEC-230

The row said *"pick one, justify it, and pin it"*. I censused the artifact instead of arguing
from the trigger dict: the captures already on disk are **448,202 bytes / 34 records** spanning
**three** `trigger_event` values (STREAM_DROP 13, SLOW_QUERY 15, TX_STALL 6) and carrying **one
key set across all 34**. The invariant that corpus has is key **homogeneity** — the key set is
already not a function of `trigger_event` — so absent-not-None would make it one and break a
reader taking a column across records. `rec["hold_site"] is None` asks the same question the
trigger dict's `in` asks, without a KeyError, for a consumer that never saw the line. This is
deliberately the **opposite** of DEC-227, and the two consumers are why.

### The guard flip, and the half a flip can silently drop

`test_top_level_keys_are_exactly_the_eight_already_on_disk` →
`test_top_level_keys_are_exactly_the_ten_after_step_2`, still an **equality**, message updated
and the removal clause kept. A flip is exactly when a guard can decay into a subset check, so
the removal half is now **its own row** (`test_the_eight_keys_already_on_disk_are_all_still_written`,
scoped to `KEYS_ALREADY_ON_DISK`) — and that row is **GREEN on both sides of the commit**, so
red→green is *no evidence for it at all*. It is scored by mutation instead: dropping each of the
ten keys from the shipped module, `py_compile`-checking every mutant so a "caught" cannot come
from a file that no longer parses, with a byte-identical restore per mutation.

### Two harness bugs my own assertions caught, both of which would have printed a fake result

1. `"delay_ms":` also sits at a 12-space indent inside `check_trigger`'s `_event` dict, so the
   first mutation harness matched it twice and would have mutated **the trigger** while claiming
   to score the record. The duplicate-match assertion caught it; the fix scopes every mutation
   to `capture_snapshot`'s own `record = {` span.
2. Everything writes through `pathlib.write_text`, never a shell redirect — zsh `noclobber` has
   silently eaten a `>` twice in this loop and turned a non-tautology check into a tautology.

### Scope fence, verified at the AST rather than read off the diff

`_run_cmd`, `_dry_run_fallback`, `check_trigger`, `extract_live_connections` and
`get_plex_fd_count` are **byte-identical** to `HEAD`'s source segments; `subprocess.` sites 4→4,
`_run_cmd(` sites 5→5, loop statements 2→2. So `task-1786124338-5963` and
`task-1786128505-658f` stay **unarmed** — this row adds no subprocess and no loop. The analyzer
is untouched; 2b and 2c own it.

### Fixtures

**Reused** from `TestTxHeldAndLiveConnections` rather than retyped — a second copy of a
"verbatim" literal is a second chance to mistype one into a synthesised fixture. Re-verified by
set membership against the 10,673-line corpus at this turn: all four used literals are
byte-identical members. Nothing is read from `/tmp` at test time.

### One thing the row asked for that I could not give it as specified

Acceptance 3 says the 06:37:39.377 `(4 live)` line writes `live_connections` — it does, 4, and
`delay_ms` 4977.0. But no **real** line reaches the both-fields-at-once world: **0 of the 42**
`Held transaction` lines in the corpus carry a `(N live)` count. LEG B of the no-flag harness
covers it with a synthesised trigger and says so, so the independence of the two fields is
measured without a fixture pretending to be real.

The corpus census the whole DEC-230 argument rests on I recomputed rather than quoted:

### The leg no earlier hat ran, and the one thing it found

The Critic's homogeneity leg put four **new** triggers into one file and got 1 key set with a
clean bracket column read. I drove the shipped CLI into an output directory **pre-seeded with
three genuine eight-key production records** lifted out of
`/tmp/plex-logs/plex_blip_diagnostics_20260807.jsonl` — the actual CT 110 shape, because the
deployed unit writes `--output-dir /var/log/plex_blip_watchdog` with a **per-day** filename
(`service.j2:9`, module `:532`) and that is where the 34 legacy records came from.

Five records, **2 distinct key sets**, and `[rec["hold_site"] for rec in recs]` raises
`KeyError`. `.get()` reads clean: `[None, None, None, 'StatisticsManager.cpp:288', None]`.

**This is not a defect of `b6d5e68` and I did not reopen the row for it.** No record shape can
retroactively add a field to records written before the field existed, and absent-not-None
would have been strictly *worse* here — 3 key sets in that file instead of 2. DEC-230's claim
is homogeneity across records *this version* writes, and that holds 10/10. What is off by one
deploy boundary is DEC-230's justification **sentence**, which says a reader can take a column
with `rec["hold_site"]`.

The window is narrow — one file mixes only if captures land on deploy-day D both before and
after the redeploy — but that day is the **Step 1 operator Demo** day
(`task-1786133422-3791`), i.e. the first capture artifact an operator will ever read. Filed as
`task-1786137337-99cf` (P3) rather than left here, because the first real *reader* is Step 9
and **Step 3 adds `sqlite` to this same record**, re-opening the same boundary a second time.
Priced and placed by the Planner; I did not promote it into a wave.

## Step 2 intra-wave routing — `queue.advance` after 2d closed (Planner, 2026-08-08, DEC-231)

Queue-contract **rule 3** fires and nothing else does: ready/open rows still exist for the
current step, so the move is *publish the next row in the same wave*, not cut a step. 2a and
2d are closed; **2b is routed**; 2c stays blocked on 2b alone and still carries Step 2's Demo.
No `plan.md` step was opened or closed at this turn.

### The false-contract check paid on 2b, and the yield was a criterion with exactly one solution

The standing rule is both halves — grep every name/file/line a routed row points at **and**
run the harnesses its acceptance cites. Everything below was measured at this turn.

**The pointers all held.** `parse_log_line` is at `analyze_plex_blips.py:55` ✓;
`format_output` at `:217` ✓; the vacuous-skip `log_path =` lines are at `:150` and `:219` ✓
(so 2b's pointers are exact, and it is `plan.md`'s Step 11 table that was one line off —
repaired there). 2a's delivered vocabulary is readable straight out of the module as 2b
instructs: `HELD_TRANSACTION_RE` (`:51`), `LIVE_CONNECTIONS_RE` (`:56`), the `TX_HELD` branch
at `:116-123`, `_basename_site`, `hold_site`, `live_connections`. Gate arithmetic still
**39** (34 shape + 3 tofu + 2 ansible), and 2b extends an existing file, so an *unchanged*
39 is the pass.

**The yield was in the harness half.** `LogEvent` (`:26-35`) is a plain `@dataclass` with
**eight** fields, **none** with a default, constructed **positionally** at **28** sites — 8
in the analyzer (`:70 :76 :78 :82 :88 :97 :99 :107`) and 20 in the test file. 2b demands
*both* that the two new fields reach "the LogEvent shape this module already uses" *and*
(acceptance 4) that the test file take **zero** deleted or modified lines. Those two demands
have exactly one joint solution, and the row named none.

Measured against a throwaway copy of both files — the tracked tree was never mutated, sha256
identical before and after (`logs/planner-2b-logevent-field-placement.py`, `.log`):

| World | Result |
|---|---|
| W0 control, as committed | exit 0, `Ran 16 tests`, OK |
| W1 appended at end, **no defaults** | exit 1, **14× `TypeError: missing 2 required positional arguments`** |
| W2 appended at end, **defaults `None`** | exit 0, `Ran 16 tests`, OK — **the only one** |
| W3 inserted mid-class, defaults `None` | exit 1, the module **does not import**: `non-default argument 'raw_line' follows default argument` — 0 tests run |

W1 is what costs the round, and it costs it *twice*: the natural repair is to add two
arguments to 20 existing test lines, which makes acceptance 4 unmeetable while looking like
the obvious fix. This is the `playbooks/plex.yml` shape (DEC-220) one level in — a criterion
that fails in a way indistinguishable from the change failing.

### Why this licensed a re-`ensure`, and why the Step 11 rows did **not** get one

`ensure` rewrites a record, and the standing rule is that only a false contract licenses
that. Acceptance 4 is **not** false — W2 exists — so this is the weaker case: a criterion
that is *satisfiable but underdetermined*, with a measured unique solution. The
discriminator that decided it: **2b is the row being routed this turn**, and a Builder with
fresh context reads the task description, never `progress.md`. A premise that decides
whether a routed row's acceptance can be met at all has to live where the Builder will see
it. The existing description was preserved **verbatim** and the measurement **appended**;
nothing measured was overwritten.

The same reasoning says the opposite for `65f2`/`99cf`/`5963`/`658f`: nobody is routed to
them this turn, so their measured descriptions stay untouched and their membership,
pricing and promotion trigger go in `plan.md` — strategy in the plan, scheduling in the
queue (mem-1786133789-8511).

### `99cf` priced and placed — Step 11, P3, and it arms at Step 9 rather than Step 3

The Finalizer handed this over explicitly. Re-derived at my own hands rather than from the
filing: **no repo code reads the capture artifact**. The only first-party hits on
`plex_blip_diagnostics` are the *writer* (`plex_blip_watchdog.py:532`) and a comment in
`test_plex_blip_watchdog.py:702`. So the row is unarmed, it is not promoted into Step 2, and
that is the third consecutive cut where a census — not the filer's prose — produced the
answer.

**P3 holds.** The other three Step 11 rows are holes in guards that have already shipped;
this one constrains a reader that does not exist yet.

**Step 3 does not arm it, and the reason is worth keeping.** Step 3 adds `sqlite` to the same
record and widens the mixed-key window a second time — but the row's fix is explicitly
reader-side, and Step 3 adds no reader. **Step 9** is the first step that reads the artifact.
Promoting on "touches the same record" would land a reader guard in a step with nothing to
guard, which is why `plan.md`'s materialization rule now says arming means *reachable*, not
*nearby*.

**One thing inside `99cf` that would have been lost in the placement** and is now named in
`plan.md`: the justification comment at `plex_blip_watchdog.py:510-511` says
`rec["hold_site"] is None` answers the question "without a KeyError". True of every record
this version writes; **false across the deploy boundary** — in the same paragraph that cites
the 34 legacy captures as its evidence. A sentence stronger than its guard, which is the
shape this loop has charged four times in `ansible/`, sitting in a comment one file over.

### Not re-litigated at this turn

`5963` and `658f` stay unpromoted: 2b adds no `_run_cmd` call and no subprocess, so neither
is armed — the arming test is *a `_run_cmd` site inside a loop*, not *the module contains a
loop*. `65f2` stays filed: 2b is charged with not adding a **third** vacuous test, not with

(`task-1786133422-3791`) remains an open operator gate and still does not block Step 2 —

## Step 2c round 2 (Builder) — DEC-234 rejection consumed, column coverage closed

The Critic's charge was guard strength, not behaviour: **nine new numeric report cells, five
pinned**. Nothing about the delivered behaviour was rebuilt — the module change in this round is
one sentence of prose.

**RED** `logs/builder-2c-r2-red.log` — `Ran 38, FAILED (failures=2)`, both subTests of
`test_the_executive_footnote_points_at_rows_and_sections_that_exist`, on
`Regex matched: 'row beneath it'`.
**GREEN** `logs/builder-2c-r2-green.log` — `Ran 38, OK`.
**GATE** `logs/builder-2c-r2-gate.log` — `GATE PASS: 39/39` exit 0 (extending, not adding, a file).
**numstat** `360/0` test, `105/2` module — acceptance 3 holds; the two module deletions are still
round 1's, both inside `format_output`.

**The four unpinned columns are pinned by CELL IDENTITY, not by a row-shaped prefix.**
`assertRegex(section, r"Holders \(TX_HELD\)\s*\|\s*4\s*\|\s*2790\.0")` cannot fail on a column it
never reaches. The new `_row_cells(section, label)` helper addresses a row by its own first cell
and returns the whole row, so each assertion is an `assertEqual` against every cell:
`["Holders (TX_HELD)", "4", "2790.0", "1110.0"]`, `["Waiters (TX_STALL)", "2", "270.0", "160.0"]`,
`["MetadataItemSetting.cpp:459", "3", "2250.0", "1110.0", "2026-08-07 06:37:36.346"]`,
`["StatisticsManager.cpp:288", "1", "540.0", "540.0", "2026-08-07 06:37:33.273"]`, and the curve
cell including its `3 counted events` denominator. The fixtures separate total from max and first
from last on both tables, and the tests assert that separation so a later fixture edit cannot
quietly make the discriminator vacuous.

**MUTATION 16/17** `logs/builder-2c-r2-mutants.log` (was 9/15). All five charged survivors are
caught, each by the new method named: M06 and M07 by `..._holders_and_waiters_rows_is_pinned`, M08
and M09 by `..._holder_attribution_rows_is_pinned`, M13 by
`..._says_so_in_words_and_promises_no_breakdown`. Two mutants added for the reworded footnote —
M16 (points at the holder row by position again) and M17 (promises the breakdown unconditionally)
— both CAUGHT. **M05 still survives and is still not charged**: ranking by event count instead of
held time orders `:459 / :409 / :288` identically at every scope measured, on the corpus and in the
fixtures, so it is an equivalent mutant and killing it would need a fixture the corpus does not
contain. The harness keeps the Critic's two controls (real suite in the mirror — `scripts/` **and**
`ansible/roles/plex/files` — asserted green after restore, tracked shas asserted unchanged).

**The footnote pointed at the wrong side.** *"The holder side is the row beneath it"* — the row
beneath `Total Lock Delay Duration` is `Lock Waiters (TX_STALL)`, the **same** side printing the
**same** 9450.0; the holder row is two beneath. It now names rows and sections by label, and the
breakdown promise is emitted only when the breakdown renders. The guard is a self-consistency
check rather than a string match: every name the footnote quotes must be a row or a heading of the
report it is printed in, at both a holders and a no-holders corpus. That kills the false promise

## Step 2 → Step 3 wave cut — `queue.advance` (Planner, 2026-08-09, DEC-238 consumed)

**Step 2 is closed, Step 3's wave is cut, and 3b is routed.** Four rows exist
(`task-1786144929-545e` 3b, `task-1786144929-67c6` 3a, `task-1786144935-a42e` 3c blocked on 3b,
`task-1786144935-b999` the operator gate). The wave table, the ten measured premises and the
arming census are in `## Active Wave` above; this entry records the three things that were
*decided* rather than merely measured.

### 1. The step's Demo is operator-only and the step is still closable — because I reproduced the red

This was the cut's one real question. `plan.md:176-178` makes Step 3's Demo `just play` against
CT 110, which the loop cannot run. Step 1 hit the same wall and the answer there was a filed gate
plus a code-complete step — but Step 1's gate wanted an artifact (real `fuser` output) that only
a deploy produces. Step 3's Demo is a **guard going red**, and a guard's red is reproducible
anywhere its condition can be evaluated.

So I evaluated it, rather than deciding whether it was evaluable.
`logs/planner-step3-wal-guard-probe.yml` under `mise exec -- ansible-playbook -i localhost, -c
local`, against a real `journal_mode=wal` database with a `truncate`d `-wal`: **RED at
43,735,168**, **GREEN at 2,097,152**, no `sqlite3`, no CT 110, no deploy. That is the step's
correctness demo, in-loop, and it moved `just play` from *the thing the step waits on* to *the
thing that confirms the fault is live*. Filed as `task-1786144935-b999`, blocking nothing.

**The park this avoids is the one this repo has paid for three times** — plex-monitoring 88–90,
plex-ramdisk twice. Seven numbered steps are untouched. An operator gate is a task.

### 2. TWO premises would each have burned a round, and both are about instruments rather than code

- **`plan.md:152` names the wrong mechanism.** "Today's audit asserts `journal_mode == 'wal'`"
  — it is a `command` + `failed_when`, not an `ansible.builtin.assert`. The only `assert` in the
  plex role near it is the **iHD/QSV gate at :364**. A Builder greps for `assert`, finds that,
  and edits the wrong task.
- **`sqlite3` is absent from the dev box and `jinja2` is absent from every interpreter the gate
  can reach.** Together these kill the two obvious harnesses: you cannot run today's audit
  locally (it dies `rc=2` on the missing binary, *not* on WAL size), and you cannot render the
  guard's `that:` expression in a shape test. The surviving instrument is `ansible-playbook`
  driving the **real** task, and the shape of the guard has to accommodate that — the size half
  must stand on `stat` alone. **That is why 3b's row says "split the guard": it is a
  measurement, not a preference.**

A Python re-spelling of the condition would have looked like the fix and been a second spelling
of the thing under test — an assertion that cannot fail with what it names, which is the defect
class this objective has now charged in four different containers.

### 3. Nothing was promoted, and the census is why — including the row that looks armed

`plan.md` Step 11 promotes a filed row when a numbered step makes its defect **reachable**. All
four were re-derived at my own hands (detail in `## Active Wave`). The load-bearing one:
**`99cf` still does not arm.** Step 3 adds `sqlite` to the very same JSONL record and re-opens
the mixed-key window a second time — it is `plan.md`'s own worked example of *reachable, not
nearby*, and the example survives contact with the step that provoked it, because the row guards
a **reader** and Step 3 adds none. Promotion trigger stays **Step 9**.

`65f2` is the cheapest filed row and is outranked again — third consecutive cut. It is deferred
rather than homeless: Step 11 is its home, and its two tests print `SKIP: Live log not present at
/tmp/plex-logs/Plex Media Server.log` inside every green gate until then. `5963` and `658f` are
unarmed *by construction* — 3a's scope fence forbids the new `_run_cmd` and `_dry_run_fallback`
call sites that would arm them — and 3a names the consequence explicitly, so a Builder who finds
it needs a probe knows the answer is to promote the row, not to ship a fifth unpinned site.

### 4. Two things handed to 3b and 3c that no row would otherwise own

- **The audit has no `-readonly`** (`tasks/main.yml:346-349`): a lock-contention audit opening
  the library database read-write. `plan.md` Step 8 makes that a shape test five steps from now.
  3b rewrites this exact invocation, so it is charged there — the cheapest time to fix a guard is
  inside the edit that makes it reachable.
- **The runbook's `03:30` `VACUUM`/`REINDEX` timer does not exist.** Zero hits for
  `VACUUM|REINDEX|03:30` anywhere in `ansible/`; `roles/plex/templates/` holds one file. The
  2026-08-04 plan proposed the timer and it was never built, and this runbook describes it as
  existing and attributes it to Plex itself. 3c is rewriting that file and pinning it with a new
  shape test — a pin that closes over §1's corrected claim while §2 still promises a timer nobody
  ships would **declare the file correct** and preserve the rot inside the guard.

### Still the Planner's, and still not repaired

`_the_blip_slice()`'s *"in log order"* docstring is false; repairing it modifies a test line,
which Step 2's acceptance forbade. Step 3 touches no analyzer file, so it is not this wave's
either. Recorded here so it stays greppable rather than becoming folklore.

### The correction that would have cost the next round, and it is in the routing artifact

`logs/planner-step3-wal-guard-probe.yml` — the probe DEC-239 proved the step closable with —
**fails on a fresh install**, and its green run could not have found it because its fixture always
had a `-wal`. `fail_msg` is templated during **task-arg finalization**, *before* the assertion is
evaluated and regardless of its verdict, so a bare `{{ plex_wal.stat.size }}` reds a host with no
WAL even though the condition short-circuits correctly:

```
fatal: [localhost]: FAILED! => {"msg": "Task failed: Finalization of task args for
'ansible.builtin.assert' failed: Error while resolving value for 'fail_msg':
object of type 'dict' has no attribute 'size'"}
```

So requirement 2's polarity trap has **two** doors, and the condition is only the first.
`| default('absent')` in `fail_msg` is load-bearing, and `test_the_fail_msg_names_the_observed_size_and_the_threshold`
pins it as such rather than as style. This is exactly why that case is run rather than reasoned about.

### The behavioural rows drive the role's OWN tasks

A hand-kept probe beside the role is a **second spelling**: flip the role's `<` to `>` and the
probe stays green. So `_run_guard()` parses the real task file, lifts the stat and assert tasks
**by containing task name**, and plays those dicts verbatim; the threshold comes from the real
`defaults/main.yml` via `-e @`. `test_the_behavioural_harness_runs_the_role_s_own_tasks` asserts
the lift is verbatim by identity against the parsed role, so an empty lift cannot read green.

### Armed, proven differentially — 9/9 (`logs/builder-3b-mutants.log`)

Mutants are edits to the **real** role, restored after each, with both controls of
`mem-1786141620-092d` (green before the first mutation, green after the final restore).

|---|---|---|

| M2 | drop the absent short-circuit | CAUGHT (absent row) |
| M3 | unguard `fail_msg`'s observed size | CAUGHT (static + absent) |
| M4 | drop `-readonly` | CAUGHT |
| M5 | stat the db instead of its WAL | CAUGHT (pairing + red row) |
| M6 | hardcode the threshold in the condition | CAUGHT |
| M7 | raise the default above the live WAL | CAUGHT |
| M8 | strip both numbers from `fail_msg` | CAUGHT |
| M9 | delete the stat + assert pair | CAUGHT (9 of 10 rows red) |

## 2026-08-09 — Step 3b round 2 (Builder), `task-1786144929-545e`, DEC-242 rejection consumed

The mechanism was not charged and is unchanged. What was charged is the one clause in the guard
that tells a human what to DO: `fail_msg` ordered *"Run the checkpoint in
docs/runbooks/plex-sqlite-maintenance.md"* and there is no checkpoint in that file — `wal_checkpoint`
appeared **nowhere in this repo**. So the moment the guard was useful it handed an operator a
pointer to nothing.

**THE REMEDY IS NOW NAMED INLINE, WHICH IS WHY THE FIX TERMINATES IN THIS ROW.** A checkpoint is
one statement, so it does not need a document:

    Checkpoint it: sqlite3 "/var/lib/.../com.plexapp.plugins.library.db" 'PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE);'
    See the journal_mode audit in docs/runbooks/plex-sqlite-maintenance.md.

Rendered, from the real red run (`logs/builder-3b-r2-demo-red-green-absent.txt`). `TRUNCATE` and
not `PASSIVE` — `PASSIVE` yields to any attached reader and Plex is always attached — and the
remedy carries **no `-readonly`**, because a checkpoint writes and a copy-pasted `-readonly`
checkpoint dies "attempt to write a readonly database". Both are pinned. The runbook is still
named, for the `journal_mode` half it really does document; **no runbook edit**, so 3c's charter
and this row's scope fence are both intact.

**THE POINTER GUARD IS A STANDING LAW, NOT A REPAIR OF ONE SENTENCE.**
`test_every_file_the_fail_msg_points_at_carries_what_it_promises` is told nothing: it extracts
whatever paths the message names, and requires of **each** that the file exists and carries every
claim word (≥4 chars) of the clause introducing it. Armed in-row by two probes worded nothing like
the shipped message — the parent's exact defect reconstructed against the same real file
(`Run the wal_checkpoint procedure in <runbook>`), and a pointer at a file that is not there. Both
must be rejected or the row fails. There is deliberately **no stopword list**: it is a knob, and
the first thing it would be turned to is exempting the word that made a promise false.

**SECOND CHARGE LANDED:** `get_checksum: false` on the WAL stat. The module default is `true`, and
every other `stat` in this repo stats a directory where that is free; this is the first stat of a
large regular file, so CT 110 would sha1 a ~42 MiB actively-appended WAL every play. Pinned by
**identity** (`is False`), not truthiness.

### Armed differentially, 10/10, against the real role (`logs/builder-3b-r2-mutants.log`)

| # | mutant | round 1 | round 2 |
|---|--------|---------|---------|
| N1 | repoint the pointer at a file that does not exist | SURVIVED | **CAUGHT** |
| N2 | delete the remediation sentence entirely | SURVIVED | **CAUGHT** |
| N3 | replace the remedy with "Reboot CT 110." | SURVIVED | **CAUGHT** |
| N4 | promise the runbook a checkpoint it does not carry (**the shipped defect**) | — | **CAUGHT** |
| N5 | copy `-readonly` into the remedy | — | **CAUGHT** |
| N6 | hardcode the db path in the remedy instead of the variable | — | **CAUGHT** |
| N7 | let the stat checksum the WAL again (`get_checksum: true`) | — | **CAUGHT** |
| N8 | drop `get_checksum`, back to the module default | — | **CAUGHT** |
| C1 | CONTROL invert the comparison | CAUGHT | **CAUGHT** |
| C2 | CONTROL drop `default()` on the observed size | CAUGHT | **CAUGHT** |

`control-before: GREEN (13/13)`, `control-after: GREEN (13/13)`, restore **byte-identical**,
survivors **0/10**.

### Acceptance, re-run not carried over

- `just test` **GATE PASS 40/40**, the file collected by name at `logs/` line 633 and green
  **13/13** (was 10/10 — three rows added; the gate counts FILES, so 40/40 is unchanged and 3c's
  pass is still 41/41, DEC-240 holds).

| probe | result |
|---|---|
| WAL exactly `8388608` | **RED** — `<` makes the threshold itself over, so the guard has no silent equal-to gap |

The check-mode row is the one worth keeping: the size half is check-mode safe because `stat` and
`assert` both support it. The **pre-existing** `sqlite3` command task above it does not declare
`check_mode: false`, so under `--check` it is skipped and `plex_sqlite_wal.stdout` is undefined.
That is inherited from `5c9a830`, is not this row's regression, and is not charged here — but it
is the journal-mode half's problem and 3c or `48bd` will be standing next to it.

### Verification, re-run rather than carried

  (6 ERROR + 6 FAIL), 0 after.

### The record shape, resolved by CENSUS rather than by symmetry

plan.md:165-166's "the sqlite block is omitted" is ambiguous. Censused
`/tmp/plex-logs/plex_blip_diagnostics_*.jsonl` at this turn: **34 records, 448,202 bytes, THREE
trigger_event values (STREAM_DROP 13, SLOW_QUERY 15, TX_STALL 6), exactly ONE top-level key set.**
Homogeneity is the only invariant that corpus has, so **both levels are always present** and a file
that is not there reads `None`. The same reader argument that made 2d choose always-present-`None`
applies one level down: `rec["sqlite"]["wal_bytes"]` as a column would `KeyError` on exactly the
records a missing WAL makes interesting.

`None` is **not** a spelling of zero, and that is load-bearing: a checkpointed WAL genuinely
measures 0 bytes and is *healthy*, while an absent one means WAL mode is off or was never entered.
`test_an_absent_wal_is_not_a_zero_byte_wal` refuses to let those collapse — and mutant **C7**
(unreadable → `0`) is caught by it.

### Mutation score — per column, and the removal half

**Columns 11/11 caught, 0 survived**, positive control **green** (renaming the local changes
nothing), every restore byte-identical (`sha256 f23db6fe7467…`), `__pycache__` cleared and the
suite run with `-B`, every mutant asserting its own edit applied exactly once.

**The removal half, which is green on both sides and cannot be scored red→green:** each of the
eleven top-level keys dropped from the SHIPPED module. Equality row red **11/11**; removal row red
on exactly the **8/11** already on disk, green on the three added later. That is the shape the row
asked for, measured.

**C9 survived the first pass and was armed, not noted.** Deleting `and db_bytes` from the ratio
left every other row green — and the fault it hides is a `ZeroDivisionError` raised *inside*
`capture_snapshot`, which loses the whole snapshot. Reachable two ways (SQLite leaves a zero-byte
database during creation; a database removed without its sidecars orphans the `-wal`). Now pinned
by `test_a_zero_byte_database_beside_a_live_wal_does_not_divide`.

### Two defects in my own first cut, both found by measurement

1. **Positional selection.** `matched_dbs[0]` is a coin flip — glob order is filesystem order — so
   a row that just creates three files scores it by luck. The test hands the match set over in a
   *controlled* order with the database last, which makes C10 deterministic.
2. **Excluding sidecars ≠ picking the database.** `not endswith(("-wal","-shm"))` still admits any
   other `.db*` neighbour the pattern sweeps up — `-journal` from a rollback-mode database, or an
   operator's `.db.bak`. Selection is now `endswith(".db")`, the one thing only the base does, and
   C11 pins it.

### The licensed flip, both halves

`TOP_LEVEL_KEYS` 10 → 11; `:691` renamed to `..._the_eleven_after_step_3` and `:1159` to
`..._the_same_eleven_...`. **Scored by census, since a green suite cannot see a stale name:**
`grep -nEi '(^|[^a-z])ten([^a-z]|$)'` over the shipped test file returns the **empty set** (3 sites
at HEAD, all three licensed). The `:447` comment now spells the history in digits.

**One correction to the routing's prediction, in the safe direction.** DEC-249 expected `:1159` to
"go green automatically the moment `:462` widens". Measured: it goes **RED** when the constant
widens ahead of the module, and green once the module ships — so it is a genuine red→green like
`:691`, not a silent pass. The census still does the real work for the rename.

### Fences, verified rather than asserted

`tasks/main.yml`, `docs/runbooks/`, `scripts/analyze_plex_blips.py` and
`scripts/test_plex_wal_guard_shape.py` all **UNTOUCHED** (`git diff --quiet` per path). `_run_cmd`
is still **4** call sites and `_dry_run_fallback` still **3** (AST-derived), so `5963` and `658f`
stay unarmed and `TestModuleLatencyBoundIsTrue` is green 4/4 — the header's 8.0s bound is untouched
because three `os.stat` calls are not a probe.

### Demo — real CLI, `--dry-run` NOT set on either run (`logs/builder-3a-demo.log`)

- **A** 40.00 MiB db + **43,735,168 B (41.71 MiB)** `-wal` + 32 KiB `-shm` → record carries the true
  counts, `MATCH` against ground truth sampled at capture time, `wal_ratio 1.0427 > 1` (the WAL is
  *larger* than the database — the pathology plan.md names), 11 top-level keys, round-trips.
- **B** `-wal` deleted → the snapshot is **still written**, 11 keys, `wal_bytes`/`wal_ratio` null,
  `db_bytes`/`shm_bytes` still true. A nice-to-have did not kill a capture.

`zsh` noclobber silently re-ran a stale demo script mid-turn (`cat >` refused an existing path and
the *old* file executed). Caught by reading the output, not the exit code. The suite's own module
swaps go through `pathlib.write_text` for exactly this reason.

`scripts/test_plex_wal_guard_shape.py` re-run at the delivered tree: **PASS 14/14**, and the
pointer row's `probes_caught` for *"promises a remedy the target does not carry"* is still **1**
(`logs/builder-3c-wal-guard-after.log`). The runbook contains **zero** occurrences of

Why A. The remedy is *already* inline in the `fail_msg` (that was DEC-244's whole point: the
runbook was a pointer at nothing, and the fix was to stop pointing and start saying). Copying it
here would put the same three ordered steps in two places, buy an operator nothing they do not

instead names *where* the remedy lives — `ansible/roles/plex/tasks/main.yml:411-421` — and says
the reclaim step is inert while Plex is up, which is the part an operator gets wrong.

silent 14 → 1 two files away. If that row re-arms the probe on a different word, this check

`logs/builder-3c-differential.py` `git show ee7c826:<runbook>` into a tmp path, points the shipped

only — and a NEW row `test_every_flag_argparse_requires_is_one_the_unit_passes` asserts

### CHARGE 2 — the suffix sentence was false, and the true reason is a different consumer

Re-measured at my own hands rather than carried (`logs/builder-4b-r2-suffix.log`, 2/2, two real

| | `db_bytes` | `wal_bytes` | `shm_bytes` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| stat truth | 4112384 | 4144752 | 32768 |
| `--db-pattern ...db*` | 4112384 | 4144752 | 32768 |
| `--db-pattern ...db` | 4112384 | 4144752 | 32768 |

**R1** — identical, so the shipped sentence ("dropping the `*` narrows the capture off
`-wal`/`-shm`, and `_wal_state` is fed by this very glob") is false: `_wal_state` is handed
`_base_db(matched_dbs)` and derives the sidecars by string concatenation.

is also `targets` for `fuser -v`/`lsof`, so the live writer holding the `-wal` is **reported by
the probes with the suffix and invisible without it**. The pin stays; the sentence is replaced
with that, and the `DB_PATTERN_SUFFIX` comment repeating the false claim is corrected in the same
edit. Both new citations into `plex_blip_watchdog.py` are by **anchor**, not by line
(`task-1786157581-f7a7`).

### WHERE I ADDED SOMETHING — the collector's READ path, which no hat in this row ran

Every hat scored what the role hands **systemd**. Nobody scored what node-exporter can **open**. The
role's own comment states the join in one sentence ("node-exporter scrapes it as its OWN uid, so
`other` needs r-x on the directory or the collector cannot open the file") and pins the directory at
0755. That chain has three links and the role pins one. `logs/finalizer-4c-collector-read-path.py`,
**8/8**:

- **The file's mode is the UNIT's umask.** `write_textfile_metrics` is `open()` + `os.replace`, so
  the `.prom` lands at `0666 & ~umask`. Driven with the REAL CLI, `--dry-run` NOT set, one real
  `TX_HELD` trigger: **0644, 1512 bytes** at systemd's default 0022 — the control arm, and the
  evidence the path works today.
- **One line moves it.** The same CLI under 0077 — what a single `UMask=0077` in
  `plex_blip_watchdog.service.j2` produces — writes **0600 root:root**, and the 0755 directory the
  role does pin cannot rescue it. node-exporter runs as the packaged unit's own uid, so it gets
  EACCES, the series go ABSENT, and `mem-1786159649-6e78` measured that an absent series cannot match
  design §5.3's `==`/`!=` expressions. Silent by construction — `cb29`'s shape one artifact over.
- **Nothing sees it.** With `UMask=0077` on the unit, `test_plex_watchdog_unit_shape.py` stays 7/7
  and `test_plex_node_exporter_shape.py` stays 9/9 (both re-run inside the harness, with the
  unmutated run as the control). The template is restored byte-for-byte in a `finally` and the
  restore is asserted, not assumed. Filed `task-1786167833-1152`, P3.

**AND A PREDICTION OF MINE WAS WRONG, which is the row worth keeping.** I expected ansible's `file`
module to leave the INTERMEDIATE `/var/lib/node_exporter` at the deploying process's umask — 0700
under 0077, which would block the exporter's uid from ever reaching the 0755 leaf. I asked real
ansible instead of arguing it: `ansible localhost -m ansible.builtin.file -a "path=<three levels
deep> state=directory mode=0755"` under `umask 0077` gives the intermediate **0755** as well.
Ansible applies the requested mode all the way down; that link is the role's decision after all. The
harness row is flipped to the true claim rather than deleted (`plex-runbook-guard-polarity-flip`).
`mem-1786167847-8b34`.

Rendered config parses and defines exactly the modules 5c will name | `test_blackbox_modules_are_the_two_token_free_probes` (renamed at 5b to `test_blackbox_modules_are_the_three_plex_probes` when the sessions module made "two token-free" false — the row is left as the record of what was true at 5a); M16/M17/M18/M19/M20 kill; real binary `--config.check` rc=0 |

**The mode is 0640, not the 0644 the row ordered, and that is a measurement.** `prom/*` is not one
identity: `prom/prometheus:v3.12.0` declares `USER nobody` (uid 65534), `prom/blackbox-exporter:
v0.28.0` declares no user and runs as **root** (`logs/builder-5a-image-identity.log`). So 0640
root:root is readable to the process, publishes nothing, and is already the mode row `5b`'s token
needs — `plan.md` premise 4's mode collision is dissolved rather than deferred, and 5b changes a
module rather than a mode (DEC-282). Driven both ways on the real image: uid 0 reads the mount at
`RestartCount 0`, `--user 65534` gets `Permission denied`.

**20 mutants, 20 killed, 3 controls held.** Two of the kills were my own defects first: `yaml.
safe_load` accepted a duplicated key the real exporter refuses at config load (`_StrictLoader` is
the repair, DEC-284), and `fail_if_not_ssl` — the field the template calls the one that
distinguishes the two modules — was unguarded until `tls_required`. The controls are what make the
row a set of relations: renaming the handler on both sides, moving the container-side path on both
sides, and **inserting 5b's `{{ vault_plex_token }}`**, all stay green.

**Real exporter, which no acceptance criterion required** (`logs/builder-5a-live-probes.log`):
`probe_success 1` against a live 200, `probe_success 0` against a dead port, `probe_success 0` for
the proxied module against plain HTTP, and `module=<unknown>` → **HTTP 400 "Unknown module"** — the
join that makes 5c block on 5a, measured rather than repeated. `--config.check` also caught what no
guard would have: `no_follow_redirects` is deprecated in v0.28.0 and warns at load; the delivered
file uses `follow_redirects: false`.

**For 5c:** `BLACKBOX_MODULES` is the tuple both sides read — `params.module` must be scored
against it, never as literals.

**Correcting the round-1 entry above, which this entry does not edit.** That entry says

for it was false. `logs/builder-5a-r2-tls-differential.log` re-drives the whole differential at
this parent on the real `prom/blackbox-exporter:v0.28.0`, with the two modules loaded out of
`blackbox.yml.j2`'s own bytes and one field changed per arm:

| row | module | target | `probe_success` |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | as shipped | `https://expiredhost` (notAfter 2026-06-01) | 0 |
| E2 | minus `fail_if_not_ssl` | same | **0** |
| E3 | `insecure_skip_verify: true` | same | **1** |
| F1-F3 | all three | `https://tlshost` (valid) | 1, 1, 1 |
| G1 | as shipped | `http://plainhost` | 0 |
| G2 | minus `fail_if_not_ssl` | same | **1** |
| H3a | direct as shipped | `http://plainhost` | 1 |
| H3b | direct **+** `fail_if_not_ssl` | same | **0** |

E1 == E2 is the whole charge: the field does nothing for an expired certificate. F1-F3 are the
control that makes E readable — all three read 1 against a valid cert, so E is a differential and
not an everything-is-zero artefact. Renewal is carried by `insecure_skip_verify` (E3) and by
nothing else; what `fail_if_not_ssl` actually buys is the plain-HTTP refusal (G1/G2).

`cert_verified` (present **and** explicitly `False`, DEC-287, so H2 scores like H1), and
`direct_accepts_plain` — the direct module must NOT carry `fail_if_not_ssl`, because H3b measures
that the field which is correct one module down makes this one report 0 **for ever**. The
docstring's false renewal sentence is replaced rather than softened, and the template's two
comments are rewritten to say what each field buys; the "NOT PINNED, deliberately" census is now
complete (six fields) instead of illustrative, because a partial list reads as a claim about the
fields it omits.

**5 killed, 0 survivors, 3 controls held** (`logs/builder-5a-r2-mutants.log`), same battery and
same row ids as the RED log so the two diff line for line. Every mutant reds on its OWN field, and

**My first instrument was wrong and the log says so.** It excluded every line starting with

irrelevant.

**CHARGE 1 — the census is now the thing the test reads.** `BLACKBOX_{TOP,MODULE,HTTP,TLS}_KEYS`
plus `_blackbox_unknown_fields` walk the document at four levels and score each by SET DIFFERENCE
— `keys_allowed`'s idiom applied to the config instead of the compose service — and `fields_known`
folds into `ok` and PRINTS the offending dotted path. The allow-list is exactly the fields the
clause pins plus the six its docstring declared "NOT PINNED, deliberately", so the prose census
stopped being prose. The walk is total over malformed documents (a level that is not a mapping is
skipped, never walked), because a second reader crashing on `http:`-as-a-scalar is the shape round
2 was opened to repair.

**CHARGE 2 — the crashed reader `_no_duplicate_keys` reintroduced.** `key in seen` raises
`TypeError` on a YAML complex key, which is not a `yaml.YAMLError` and so escapes the clause's own
`except`. Caught by BEHAVIOUR (`try: key in seen / except TypeError`) rather than by an isinstance
list of unhashable types, and re-raised as the `ConstructorError` the reader already handles; the
message carries the node TAG (`!!seq`, the binary's own noun) because a `deep=False` sequence
reprs as `[]` and names nothing.

walk.

**Task:** `task-1786175878-f752`, key `code-assist:plex-blip-manual-triage:step-05:blackbox-sessions-token`.
Parent `d0cb5b0`. Gate **43/43 → 43/43**, shape suite **46/46 → 47/47** (+1 clause) — read as the
relation at this row's own parent, never as a literal.

### What shipped

| Artifact | Change |
|---|---|
| `templates/blackbox.yml.j2` | the third module, `plex_sessions`: `/status/sessions` with `headers: {X-Plex-Token: "{{ vault_plex_token \| default('') }}"}`, `timeout: 30s`, `valid_status_codes: [200]`, no `fail_if_not_ssl` |
| `scripts/…_shape.py` | `BLACKBOX_SESSIONS_MODULE` + `BLACKBOX_TOKEN_HEADER`; `headers` -> `_bb_string_map` in the census; `RENDER_OWNER` column on all five `RELOAD_CONTRACT` rows + `_render_task_scalar`; new clause `test_blackbox_sessions_probe_carries_the_vault_token`; `…_are_the_two_token_free_probes` renamed to `…_are_the_three_plex_probes` |

### RED first, on the field the clause names

`logs/builder-5b-red.log`, before the template gained the module: `FAIL: 2/47`, and the sessions
clause printed `module_defined=False, header_key=False (plex_sessions.http.headers keys=[],
want=['X-Plex-Token']), token_from_vault=False (value=None)`. The field, not "something failed".

### The census predicate is a MEASUREMENT, and `headers` is a trap the other way round

`logs/builder-5b-headers-probe.log`, 23 documents at `--config.check` on
`prom/blackbox-exporter:v0.28.0`. The row was warned that three predicates in this census were
already traps; this one is a trap in the OPPOSITE direction from `prober`:

    headers: {X-Plex-Token: abc} / {} / two keys / ~     rc=0
    headers: {X-Plex-Token: 5 / true / ~ / 1.5}          rc=0
    headers: {200: abc} / {on: abc}                      rc=0
    headers: abc / 5 / [X-Plex-Token]                    rc=1
    headers: {X-Plex-Token: [a]} / {X-Plex-Token: {a:b}} rc=1

`prober: 3` LOADS because go-yaml fills a Go **string** from a raw scalar — but this field is
`map[string]string`, so `_bb_scalar` would have been a **FALSE-ACCEPT** on `headers: abc`, rc=1
and a crash loop under `restart: unless-stopped`. The raw-scalar rule applies one level DOWN
instead, to the map's values. Hence `_bb_string_map`: a mapping whose values are each `_bb_scalar`,
plus `None`, because a bare `headers:` loads and redding it would false-RED a document the binary
accepts. Mutants `C2`/`C3` are those two rows; `C1` deletes the census entry and the SHIPPED
template reds as `unknown=[…headers]` — `5a`'s declared L1 cost, paid in the same commit.

### The real harness: rendered by Ansible, then driven to the WIRE (`logs/builder-5b-render-and-wire.py/.log`)

The play is GENERATED from the role's own task body — `src`/`dest`/`owner`/`group`/`mode` read out
of `tasks/main.yml`, never re-spelled, so the harness reports the mode the role writes.

    [A] renders rc=0 in BOTH token states — undefined (today's state) and set
    [B] both rendered documents --config.check rc=0 on the pinned binary
        on-disk 640 root:root, world-readable=0, unprivileged `cat` -> Permission denied
        rendered header: X-Plex-Token: ""   /   X-Plex-Token: "zzTESTTOKENnotreal1234"
    [C] the origin's transcript, from a live exporter on the shipped bytes:
        GET /status/sessions
        User-Agent: Blackbox-Exporter/0.28.0
        X-Plex-Token: zzTESTTOKENnotreal1234      <- the RENDERED value, on the wire

`| default('')` is what makes today's undefined state landable, and leg B is where that stops
being an argument: the token-undefined render loads. Contained per the standing rule — private
docker network, targets by container name, nothing published, and the harness prints that first.

### My own harness was wrong THREE times, and each is the entry worth keeping

1. **It printed the template's own COMMENT as the rendered header line.** `if "X-Plex-Token" in l`
   matched the prose four lines above the field. This template quotes its field spellings in
   comments more than any other in the repo — the recorded trap, and I walked into it inside the
   same row that records it. Fixed to `l.strip().startswith("X-Plex-Token:")`.
2. **Leg C reported "no header sent" when nothing had been sent to it.** An `nc`-in-a-loop origin
   answered correctly — the exporter read `probe_success 1`, `probe_http_status_code 200` off it —
   but blackbox does not close the connection, so `timeout 5` killed `nc` before it flushed and the
   transcript came back EMPTY. **An empty transcript is indistinguishable from the defect leg C
   exists to detect**: the harness was reporting its own plumbing as the finding. The POSITIVE
   CONTROL is what exposed it (a third container's request WAS recorded, ruling out "this origin
   records nothing"), and the fix is an origin that records and then sends `Connection: close`.
3. **It drove `/probe` by `docker exec`ing into the exporter**, silently depending on what tooling
   that image ships. Now a third container, like the control.

### 19 mutants, 19 as expected (`logs/builder-5b-mutants.py/.log`)

`(pattern, replacement, want_count)` with the count asserted EXACTLY — and it earned that
immediately: `L1` anchored on `follow_redirects: false`, which substituted **3** times because all
three modules carry it, and the assertion stopped the battery instead of scoring a mutant that
edited the wrong module. Re-anchored on the proxied module's own unique field.

`H1` key renamed, `H2` a second header, `T1` a literal token, `T2` the `default('')` filter
dropped, `T3` a second vault variable, `L1` a literal header under a DIFFERENT module (the arm
`token_from_vault` structurally cannot see), `S1` `fail_if_not_ssl: true`, `C1`-`C3` the census,
`N1` the module dropped from the tuple `5c` reads, `W1` 0644, `W2` a symbolic mode, `O1`/`O2` the
owner column, `P1` the token smuggled into `prometheus.yml.j2`. Controls `K1`/`K2` — a legal
duration and a legal status-code widening — stay GREEN, so this is a vocabulary and not a freeze.

**`W3` is the row worth reading, and it corrected ME.** The acceptance's own named control is that
`0600` must not false-RED the world bit. It does not — but the SUITE still reds under `0600`, from
an OLDER clause: `RELOAD_CONTRACT` pins this render's mode as the exact digits `0640`. My control
was scoring the suite when the acceptance is about the arm. Re-scored per-arm
(`ARM_OK:world_bit_withheld`), and the two pins are complementary rather than redundant: the
contract says which mode the role CHOSE, this row's arm says which PROPERTY of it carries the
secret, and only the second survives a deliberate future move to 0600.

### The owner column, and the sentence it is careful NOT to say

`RENDER_OWNER` on all five rows — ONE spelling, five readers, because five literals that agree is
the class this suite has charged on `--output-dir`, on the watchdog program path and on the
node-exporter binary. Armed: `O1` prints
`owner="65534":root (want root:root)` with `mode="0640"` and every other hop still green.

**Readability is NOT the reason, and the clause says so in its own output.** `Config.User` is empty
on this image and `id -u` is `0` (re-measured at my own parent, `logs/builder-5b-headers-probe.log`,
not carried from the routing's log). Five owner/mode pairs `:ro` into it are READ=ok on all five —
uid 0 bypasses the bits — so there is no pair a guard could arm "the container could not read it"
against. The clause prints `runs_as_uid=0 — readability is NOT what any mode here buys, uid 0
bypasses the bits; measured at <log>`. This closes the `RELOAD_CONTRACT` half of
`task-1786187620-633b`.

### Charge 2 — the reader broke on EVERY legal prologue, not only `---`

Four measured through real ansible-core 2.21.1 into `prom/blackbox-exporter:v0.28.0
--config.check`. Three are documents the binary TAKES at rc=0: a bare `---`, a `%TAG` directive +
`---`, and a UTF-8 BOM. On all three the shipped guard red printing three statements that are false
of a 48-line header. `_leading_comment_block` now scans to the first KEY — prologues skipped, a BOM
stripped, anything unrecognised STOPS it — and returns the line it stopped at, so the clause prints
what the reader could not see instead of an absence dressed as a fact about the prose.

### plan.md's third test bullet is CORRECTED, not implemented

It asks for `scrape_timeout < scrape_interval`. Measured on `prom/prometheus:v3.12.0` rather than
cited: **equality LOADS** (60s vs 60s is rc=0), only `>` is refused. The repo's shipped `<=`
(`timeout_fits`, "does not exceed") is the correct relation and is what shipped. The Planner's
premise 2 called this in advance; this row is the measurement behind it.

### Two things this row did that its record did not ask for, both in `decisions.md`

* **DEC-313** — `_relabel_triplet` is applied to all FOUR jobs, so `pve-exporter` gains the
  `instance` hop it has carried since Step 2 with nothing pinning it. Licensed by the row's own
  "apply it to all four jobs". Falsified at the wire: without that hop everything is green, rc=0
  and `probe_success 1`, and all three probes land on `instance=blackbox-exporter:9115`.
* **DEC-314** — `scrape_timeout: 35s` on the sessions job, because `blackbox.yml.j2` already ships
  two sentences that are claims about this row's numbers, and Prometheus' 10 s default would make
  that module's argued-for 30 s unreachable.

`docker_host_plex_url` is `defaults/main.yml:74` (the record says `:60`) and
`PROM_DEFAULT_SCRAPE_TIMEOUT` is `scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py:1663` (the record says
`:1647`). Nothing shipped cites either number: both are reached through constants
(`PLEX_URL_VAR`, `PROM_DEFAULT_SCRAPE_TIMEOUT`), which is what `task-1786157581-f7a7` asks for.

### What is NOT held here, said plainly

Design §5.3's **exact-match** job selectors (`job="blackbox-plex-identity"`,
`job="blackbox-plex-sessions"`) have no far end to cite: Step 7 does not exist. What is pinned is
the shared `blackbox-plex` prefix that `job=~"blackbox-plex.*"` needs. A rename that keeps the
prefix still breaks two alert rules and nothing in this repo can see it — that is Step 7's row.

## Step 5c round 2 (Builder) — DEC-316's charge repaired by ARMING, not re-wording

Suite `50/50`, gate `43/43`. One file touched: `scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py`.

### The sentence was false and it is now a check

`_relabel_triplet` shipped "A later entry overwriting `__param_target` with a literal is green
here, **and it fails loudly at deploy**." Re-driven at my own hands — real
`prom/prometheus:v3.12.0` + `prom/blackbox-exporter:v0.28.0` + a stub origin, on renders of the
shipped templates (`logs/builder-5c-r2-wire.py`, `.log`):

    W4   promtool rc=0   up=1   probe_success=0 on instance="http://origin/identity"

Nothing is loud, and `PlexUnreachable` fires an outage alert about the healthy origin the CONTROL
row probes at `probe_success 1`. Reproduced DEC-316's C3/C4/C5/C6 rather than adopting them.

### Three rows the charge did not have, and one of them changed the design of the fix

* **W5** — a SECOND `source_labels` member: rc=0, `probe_success 1`,
  `instance="http://origin/identity;http"`. Not a new field, the same silence, so the arm compares
  the source list WHOLE.
* **W7** — a fourth entry `action: labeldrop` / `regex: instance`: rc=0, `up 1`,
  `probe_success 1`, and `instance` back to `blackbox-exporter:9115`. It writes no `target_label`,

* **W6** — `action: replace` written out explicitly: byte-identical to CONTROL. This is the
