**W3 is the boundary control**: `action: labeldrop` on a hop is `promtool` rc=1. It was never
silent, so the class is the fields that leave a VALID config — without that row the paragraph
would be claiming a hole where the runtime already stands.

## Step 5c round 3 (Builder) — DEC-318 rejection consumed

`task-1786175907-d338`. Suite `51/51`, gate `43/43`, one file touched
(`scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py`), plus two regenerated logs.

### The charge holds, and the door it proved is short by one field

DEC-318: the comment above `_RELABEL_HOP_FIELDS` justified the exclusions with "everything else in
a relabel entry — `regex`, `action`, `modulus`, `separator`, a second `replacement` — changes what
the hop writes or whether it writes at all". Every one of those has a default, and a default
written out explicitly is a no-op the allow-list reds.

The charge named FIVE fields. The excluded set is **SIX**: `source_labels` is excluded on the
`dial` hop and nowhere else, so `source_labels: []` — the default empty list — is a sixth working
document nobody had counted. Fifteen documents, not five and not one: five excluded fields on each
of `carry` and `keep`, five on `dial`.

### The repair is a relation, not a better sentence

A prose count is a measurement sentence with nothing arming it, which is the defect this row has
now been rejected for three times. So the enumeration became two constants — `_PROM_RELABEL_FIELDS`
(the seven fields a Prometheus `relabel_config` entry may declare) and `_RELABEL_NOOP_SPELLING`
(each excluded field's no-op spelling) — and `test_relabel_allow_list_prices_every_field_it_excludes`
holds them against `_RELABEL_HOP_FIELDS`, printing the count at every run.

RED first: the clause was written carrying EXACTLY the charge's five fields and reds naming
`source_labels` unpriced (`logs/builder-5c-r3-red.log`).

### Both legs from ONE mutation, which is what makes the table readable

`logs/builder-5c-r3-price.py/.log`. The hop lines are Jinja-free, so the same edit to the shipped
template drives the guard and the wire: real ansible-core render → real `prom/prometheus:v3.12.0`
(promtool and the running server) + `prom/blackbox-exporter:v0.28.0` + a stub origin, `--internal`
network, nothing published. Each row is scored against a verdict written down before the run.

### Mutants — 9/9, and two of them are controls

`logs/builder-5c-r3-mutants.py/.log`: an allow-list typo (`target_labels`), the declared door out
walked through without the price following it, a field deleted from the Prometheus set, a future
field added, the `action` row deleted, and a row that names one field while demonstrating another
— six kills. `C1` (the field tuple reordered) and `C2` (`separator: ';'` in the other quoting) stay
GREEN, so the clause is about the relation and not the bytes. `V7` deletes the registration line
and the total moves 51 → 50, which is what says the clause runs at all.

### Second charge, both halves

* `logs/builder-5c-r2-arms.log` regenerated. Its `run()` read the verdict with a `$`-anchored
  regex and the suite's FAIL spelling carries a trailing "checks failed", so all 13 kills printed
  `NO VERDICT *** SURVIVED ***` above bodies reading `FAIL: 1/50`. Anchor removed, and the harness
  now copies the guard under test in from the working tree, so the log is about the bytes this
  round ships: 13 KILLED, the 2 spelling controls green.
* `_relabel_entry_fields`' docstring said `%` where YAML's merge key is `<<`. Behaviour was always
  right (`_yaml_key` refuses it, so it lands in `unreadable`).

### One sentence nobody charged, the same class one caller over

`_relabel_triplet`'s own defect text told the reader that a field beside the copy "decides whether
the hop fires and what it writes, and every spelling of that leaves a config Prometheus loads".
Both halves are false in the same direction as the charged comment — the no-op spellings decide
nothing, and `action: labeldrop` is `promtool` rc=1. Rewritten in the same commit.

`logs/builder-5d-premises.{sh,log}`, pinned `prom/prometheus:v3.12.0`, four container rows plus

So the TSDB is already inside the `prometheus-data` volume and **no `--storage.tsdb.path` is

does print `[DEPRECATED] … use the storage.tsdb.retention.time field in the config file instead`.

`logs/builder-5d-render.{py,log}` — the template rendered through Jinja with the role's own

## Step 5d — round 2, the two charges of DEC/review.rejected

The rejection's basis: **the row paid for the far door and left the near one open.** The value-pin
on `--storage.tsdb.retention.time=90d` is undone by the SIBLING flag in the same `command:` list,
not only by another spelling of the pinned flag.

### The charges, reproduced at my own hands first (`logs/builder-5d-r2-redfirst.log`)

* **A control** — delivered tree, unmodified: `PASS 52/52`, clause prints `keeps its TSDB for 90d`.
* **B charge 1** — `--storage.tsdb.retention.size=1MB` added to this row's own `command:` list:
  **`PASS 52/52`, still printing 90d.** The hole reproduces exactly as charged.
* **C charge 2** — `--storage.tsdb.path=data/`, the flag's OWN documented default written out:
  round 1's clause **FALSE-REDs** on a tree the binary puts in the same place as no flag at all.

### The measurements the repair rests on (`logs/builder-5d-r2-premises.log`, pinned `v3.12.0`)

The knob census — every `--help` flag matching `/retention/`, driven, not read:

| row | argv | result |
|-----|------|--------|
| X1 | `--storage.tsdb.retention=90d` | rc=1 `unknown long flag` — LOUD |
| X2 | `--storage.tsdb.retention.percentage=50` | rc=1 `unknown long flag` — LOUD (the log line prints the field; no flag sets it) |
| X3 | `--storage.agent.retention.max-time=4h` | rc=3 `can only be used in agent mode` — LOUD |
| X4/S2 | `--storage.tsdb.retention.size=1MB` beside `=90d` | **rc=0, starts, `duration=90d size=1MiB`** — the only SILENT sibling |
| S3 | size flag alone | `duration=0s size=1MiB` — size alone also disables the 15d default |

The path spellings, one live container each, `wal` located on a real bind mount:
`P0` no flag → `/prometheus/data/wal`; `P1` `data/` → same; `P1b` `data` → same;
`P1c` `/prometheus/data` → same; `P2` `/prometheus` (the image CMD value, the trap) →
`/prometheus/wal`. Zero `/deprecat/` lines in any running process.

### The repair

* `no_size_cap` — a fifth conjunct pinning `--storage.tsdb.retention.size` **ABSENT** from the same
  list, read through `_last_flag_value` like every other flag here. Absence and not a value:
  there is no disk cap this stack wants, and a later row that wants one pays by moving the pin.
  The config-file side of BOTH flags was already closed by `no_config_side_retention`.
* `tsdb_path_ok` — now **RESOLVES** rather than compares: the value is joined onto the image
  WORKDIR the way the binary joins it, so all four spellings that land the TSDB in
  `PROMETHEUS_TSDB_DIR` are admitted by one rule and `/prometheus` still reds. Its boundary is
  stated on the clause (`.` and trailing slashes collapse, `..` does not).

### Driven both ways (`logs/builder-5d-r2-bothways.log`)

`PASS 52/52` on the delivered tree; `FAIL 1/52` naming `no_size_cap=False (…='1MB')` on the size
mutant; `FAIL 1/52` naming `tsdb_path_ok=False (… resolves … to '/prometheus')` on the trap;

## Step 5d round 4 (Builder) — DEC-306 rejection consumed

Active task `task-1786175925-9c1c`, key `code-assist:plex-blip-manual-triage:step-05:prometheus-retention-90d`,
parent `f0ab57f`. One charge, one root cause, one edit.

### The charge

`_last_flag_value` matched `--flag=value` and nothing else. kingpin also accepts the two-token
`--flag value` form, and a compose `command:` **list** is exactly where that spelling is natural —
two sequence entries. Round 3's two ABSENCE-shaped conjuncts (`no_size_cap`, `tsdb_path_ok`) fail
**OPEN** on it; `retention_pinned` does not, because a value pin fails **CLOSED**.

### RED FIRST, on the FIELD (`logs/builder-5d-r4-redfirst.log`)

13 rows against the delivered guard, each mutating the shipped template's bytes and restoring them
(sha1 printed). Five holes GREEN at `PASS 52/52`, two correct trees FALSE-RED:

| row | tree | before |
|-----|------|--------|
| M2 | size cap two-token `1MB` | **52/52 green**, printing `no_size_cap=True (…=None, want absent)` |
| M4 | path two-token `/prometheus` | **52/52 green**, printing `tsdb_path_ok=True (value=None …)` |
| N1 | size cap bare, LAST entry, no value | **52/52 green** |
| N2 | size cap bare followed by another flag | **52/52 green** |
| C2 | blackbox `--config.file` two-token decoy appended | **52/52 green** (`reads_it` blind) |
| M7 | `--storage.tsdb.retention.time` + `90d` — CORRECT | **FALSE-RED** `retention_pinned=False (value=None)` |
| C1 | prometheus `--config.file` two-token — CORRECT | **FALSE-RED** `reads_it=False` |

### The repair — one reader, three consumers

A bare token equal to the flag is an occurrence whose value is the next arg, and `UNREADABLE` when
that arg is missing or is itself a flag — the fail-closed polarity this helper already took for an
opaque flag half. `UNREADABLE` is a string, so it composes with the WORKDIR join and reds there too.

### Same battery after (`logs/builder-5d-r4-mutants.log`) — 13/13 as expected

M2 → `FAIL 1/52` `no_size_cap=False (…='1MB')`; M4 → `tsdb_path_ok=False (value='/prometheus')`;
N1/N2 → `no_size_cap=False (…='<UNREADABLE ENTRY>')`; C2 → `reads_it=False` on `blackbox.yml.j2`;
M7 and C1 now **GREEN**; M2b/M3 (round 2's `=` pins) RED both ways — no spelling traded for another;
N3 RED both ways; N4 (`data/` two-token, a correct resolving spelling) green both ways; N5 (an inert
unrelated two-token flag) `52/52` both ways; M0 delivered control `52/52` both ways.

Leg B — `docker compose up -d` on those bytes: M2 is `running restarts=0` logging
`TSDB retention updated duration=90d size=1MiB`; M4 is `running restarts=0` with `wal/` and

`vars:` and the role default `/opt/stack` won) — fixed with `-e` and stated in the harness.

Gate `43/43` (`logs/builder-5d-r4-gate.log`), shape suite `PASS 52/52`, `ansible-lint --offline
--profile production` and `--syntax-check` both OK. One tracked file changed.

**Delivered.** A new `ansible/roles/docker_host/templates/plex-blip-rules.yml.j2` carrying design
§5.3's seven alerts, a render task in `tasks/main.yml` with `notify: Restart prometheus`,
`prometheus/rules` added to the extras-dirs loop, `rule_files:` in `prometheus.yml.j2` as a LITERAL
path, the `:ro` file bind in `compose.yml.j2`, and a SIXTH `RELOAD_CONTRACT` row in
`scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py`. Two new clauses
(`test_prometheus_rule_files_names_the_render`, `test_plex_blip_alert_rules_are_design_5_3`), and
`test_prometheus_render_is_world_read_only_unpaid` generalised to a two-row column.

**Gate: `GATE PASS: 43/43`** (`logs/builder-7a-gate.log`), guard `PASS: 54/54`, `ansible-lint
--offline --profile production` and `ansible-playbook --syntax-check site.yml` both inside it.

### The six premises, every one driven on the pinned `prom/prometheus:v3.12.0`

`promtool`, each direction with its own positive control (`logs/builder-7a-literal-vs-glob.log`):

| leg | config | result |
|---|---|---|
| A | `check rules` on the seven | rc=0, `SUCCESS: 7 rules found` |
| B | `check config`, LITERAL path, file present | rc=0, `SUCCESS: 1 rule files found` |
| C | `check config`, LITERAL path, file ABSENT | **rc=1**, `does not point to an existing file` |
| C2 | `check config`, GLOB over an EMPTY dir | **rc=0**, and the `N rule files found` line ABSENT |
| C3 | the same GLOB with the file present | rc=0, both SUCCESS lines |

C2 against C3 is the whole argument for the literal, and it is the acceptance's row 1.

**AND THE LOUDNESS IS `promtool`'s, NOT THE PROCESS'S** — this is the one place the wave-cut premise
needed narrowing, and the clause says so rather than inheriting the stronger reading. Driven live
(`logs/builder-7a-handler-question.log` rows D5b/D6): a literal naming a MISSING file is `running
restarts=0`, `Completed loading of configuration file`, `/api/v1/rules` → `{"groups":[]}` —
byte-for-byte the glob-over-an-empty-dir leg. The literal buys an exit code and nothing else until
something runs the validator before the handler fires, which is exactly **7c**.

### The handler question, answered by measurement (acceptance 3)

The four `RELOAD_CONTRACT` rows above this one each earned their `notify:` on ONE finding. This file
has TWO (`logs/builder-7a-handler-question.log`, `evaluation_interval: 15s`, so +40s is 2+ cycles):

| row | action | result |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | `POST /-/reload` | 403 `Lifecycle API is not enabled.` |
| D2 | edit IN PLACE, same inode, +40s | container SEES the new bytes, process serves the OLD rule set |
| D3 | ATOMIC REPLACE, new inode, +40s | the container cannot even SEE the new bytes — a single-FILE bind holds the old inode, and `os.replace` is what `ansible.builtin.template` does |
| D4 | CONTROL, `docker restart` | same container id, new inode visible, new rule set served |

D3 is the new one: a rules edit without the handler is not merely unread, it is INVISIBLE inside the
container. So `notify: Restart prometheus` is the whole delivery path, twice over.

### The mode is 0644 and it was driven on THIS file (`logs/builder-7a-rules-mode.log`)

`docker run --entrypoint id` → `uid=65534(nobody)`. At **0640 root:root** the container is
`restarting restarts=7` logging `open /etc/prometheus/rules/plex-blip-rules.yml: permission denied`
— verbatim the crash loop that emptied this stack's TSDB from 2026-05-28 to 2026-07-29 — and at
**0644** it is `running restarts=0` serving `groups=['plex-blip']`. Note the asymmetry with the legs
above: an UNREADABLE rules file is LOUD, a MISSING one is silent.

### The strongest harness available, on the REAL render (`logs/builder-7a-rendered-promtool.log`)

`ansible-playbook` with the repo inventory rendered both templates through
`ansible.builtin.template` itself; `promtool check config` over the pair mounted exactly as
`compose.yml.j2` mounts them is rc=0 with `SUCCESS: 1 rule files found` + **`SUCCESS: 7 rules
found`** (acceptance 5, on the render rather than on a hand copy of §5.3). A real container booted on
that pair is `running restarts=0` with all seven rules loaded and evaluating.

### Arming: 14 mutants, 14 killed, 0 survivors (`logs/builder-7a-mutants.py`, `.log`)

Each applied to the delivered tree, whole guard run, reverted in a `finally`; baseline and revert
both `rc=0`. M1 glob, M2 no `notify:`, M3 `dest` moves alone, M4 `rule_files:` moves alone, M5 mode
0640, M6 the meta-guard alert deleted, M7 exact job renamed, M8 `event_type` typo, M9 watchdog metric
typo, M10 regex narrowed, M11 licence sentence deleted, M12 a `for:` changed, M13 the bind mount
deleted, M14 a FOURTH `blackbox-plex-*` job joining the fan-in (the superset direction).

M14 is the half `prometheus.yml.j2`'s own 5c comment deferred in writing — "the exact-match half
arrives with the rules" — and it closes as a set equality rather than a prefix.

### What this row does NOT claim (acceptance 4)

That any of the seven FIRE. Four compare a series to a constant and cannot match an ABSENT one;
`absent()` is the door, measured at the wave cut with a positive control, and **7b** owns it. No
`labels:` and no `annotations:` are shipped or pinned either — §5.3 carries none and plan.md's
severity+summary bullet is **7d**'s obligation, not a property of the source.

`_loop_items` (`scripts/test_grafana_provisioning_shape.py:1599`) breaks at the first list line it
cannot read as an entry, so a COMMENT inside the extras-dirs `loop:` drops every grafana dir from

## Step 7a round 2 (Builder) — the `expr` bodies are held, DEC-323 rejection consumed

`task-1786225337-d829`. Round 1 was rejected on ONE charge and the charge was right: the seven
`expr:` bodies — the substance of every alert — were pinned by nothing. `PLEX_BLIP_ALERTS` held
`(alert, for)` and the four coordinate readers held metric NAMES, job labels and the `event_type`
VALUE, so no threshold, comparison operator, range window or label KEY was held anywhere.

**`ansible/` is byte-identical to `08a5b7a`** (`git diff --stat -- ansible/` is empty). The Critic
checked the document by hand and found the seven delivered exprs ARE design §5.3 today; the artifact
was right and the guard was the defect, so this round moves only `scripts/test_traefik_config_shape.py`.

### RED first, and it reproduced the charge exactly

`logs/builder-7a-r2-mutants.py` → `logs/builder-7a-r2-mutants-RED.log`, run against the guard as
delivered: **8/8 survivors at `PASS: 54/54`**, both anti-false-RED controls green.

    T1  PlexProbeSlow             > 0.5      -> > 500       R10's headline detection, inert
    T2  PlexSqliteWalOversized    > 8388608  -> x1000       the R12 leading indicator, inert
    T3  PlexUnreachable           == 0       -> == 1        INVERTED: fires when Plex is UP
    T4  PlexExporterScrapeFailing == 0       -> != 0        inverted
    T5  PlexTransactionHoldStorm  [5m]       -> [500m]      the range window
    T6  PlexSessionsProbeStalled  > 5        -> > 5000      past the 101,394 ms observed live
    T7  PlexWatchdogProbeBroken   == 0       -> == 1        THE META-GUARD, inverted
    T8  event_type="TX_HELD"      -> eventtype="TX_HELD"    the label KEY, matching nothing

Each anchored on its own unique `expr:` line, because `== 0` appears three times and a
first-occurrence edit would have scored as a guard defect in the wrong rule
(`mutate-repeated-literal-via-task-anchor`).

### GREEN — one column on a table this row already ships

`PLEX_BLIP_ALERTS` goes `(alert, for)` → `(alert, expr, for)`, and `found` is built the same way with
`" ".join(expr.split())` so rewrapping a long expr is not a false RED while every character that
carries meaning is held. `logs/builder-7a-r2-mutants-GREEN.log`: **8/8 KILLED, 10/10 agree**, both
controls (a comment edit, the same expr respelled with double spaces) still green, revert clean, and
the delivered tree still **`PASS: 54/54`** — additive, not a retarget.

**Not fake kills** (`logs/builder-7a-r2-kill-reason.log`, `mutants-that-fake-a-kill`): each mutant
reds **exactly one** clause, mine, with `alerts_are_the_design=False` and the mutated expr named in
`divergences`. Nothing collateral.

### The follow-on charge, closed in the same round: the readers are NOT dead weight

If the rules file must equal the table, can a coordinate ever fail to resolve while the table still
agrees? Yes — the two face opposite ways. The table holds the file to one agreed text; the readers
hold that text to the REST OF THE STACK. Driven as arms rather than asserted
(`logs/builder-7a-r2-readers-still-armed.py/.log`), every mutant editing a FAR END and never the
rules file: R1 `plex-exporter` renamed at the scrape config, R2 a fourth `blackbox-plex-*` job
joining the fan-in, R3 the WAL family renamed at the watchdog source, R4 the classifier's `TX_HELD`
renamed. **All four RED with `alerts_are_the_design=True` and `divergences=[]`** — the table fully
satisfied, the redness coming only from a reader — and control C1 (a scrape job no rule cites,
renamed) stays GREEN, so the readers are a join and not a checksum over `prometheus.yml.j2`.

The docstring's *"NOT PINNED, and each absence is deliberate"* list omitted the largest absence,
which is charge point 2; it now says out loud that the list is what a later hat reads. And the

## 2026-08-09 — Step 7b delivered (Builder), `task-1786225353-1b5a`

`tasks.ready` (DEC-327) consumed. The four rules that cannot match an absent series get three
`absent()` DOORS, and the row's three filed rows are each closed by id with the measurement that
closes them or re-filed with exactly what is still open.

`GATE PASS: 43/43` rc=0 (`logs/builder-7b-gate.log`), guard `PASS: 55/55`.

### ONE DOOR PER PRODUCER, NOT ONE PER RULE

All seven of design §5.3's rules compare a series to a constant, so all seven — not the four the
row's title names — are silent when their producer stops. But the absences are SHARED: the three
blackbox jobs vanish with the blackbox exporter, `up{job="plex-exporter"}` vanishes only with its
scrape config, and the watchdog's families vanish with its `.prom`. Three doors:

| door | expr | `for:` | covers |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PlexBlackboxProbesAbsent` | `absent(probe_success{job="…-identity"}) or …sessions… or …proxied…` | 5m | `PlexProbeSlow`, `PlexSessionsProbeStalled`, `PlexUnreachable` |
| `PlexExporterTargetAbsent` | `absent(up{job="plex-exporter"})` | 5m | `PlexExporterScrapeFailing` |
| `PlexWatchdogTextfileAbsent` | `absent(plex_sqlite_db_bytes)` | 10m | `PlexSqliteWalOversized`, `PlexTransactionHoldStorm`, `PlexWatchdogProbeBroken` |

`for:` is not a feel: each is at least four missed scrapes of the slowest job that feeds it, and the
guard READS the cadence out of `prometheus.yml.j2` (`_effective_scrape_pair`) rather than spelling
it. 300s vs 240s wanted, 300s vs 240s, 600s vs 60s. Raising `blackbox-plex-sessions` to `2m` reddens
`for: 5m` instead of quietly turning the door into a same-scrape tripwire.

### THE ROW'S OWN DEFECT CLASS, ONE STEP IN — and it cost the first draft

**`absent()` over a regex is silent while ANY one of the three still reports.** `absent()` returns a
sample only when its selector matches NOTHING, so the obvious one-line spelling
`absent(probe_success{job=~"blackbox-plex.*"})` is EMPTY with two of three probes gone. Driven as a
control rather than argued (`logs/builder-7b-absence-doors.log` leg C, and live at `:19092`):

    identity + sessions present, proxied job deleted
      PlexBlackboxProbesAbsent   pending, 1 alert, job="blackbox-plex-proxied"
      RegexShapedDoorControl     inactive, 0 alerts

So the door names each job separately, and `no_regex_inside_absent` refuses the other spelling
file-wide.

### THE SECOND DRAFT WAS ALSO WRONG, AND THE REAL CLI IS WHAT SAID SO

The watchdog door was first written over `absent(plex_sqlite_wal_bytes)`. `render_textfile_metrics`
OMITS a family whose measurement is null rather than publishing a zero, so running the real CLI
(legs W1/W2) settles which family is unconditional:

    W1  never triggered, WAL checkpointed   130 BYTES — plex_sqlite_db_bytes ALONE
    W2  never triggered, WAL live           3 gauges: wal + db + shm

A WAL door therefore pages on every checkpointed database, and a `probe_status` door pages on every
healthy stack that has not blipped (`39db` R5, re-measured here). `plex_sqlite_db_bytes` is the one
family present whenever the document is, so its absence IS the document's absence. The refused draft
is kept as mutant leg D: `promtool test rules` rc=1 on the SHIPPED unit file, failing in **both**
healthy legs.

### ACCEPTANCE 2 AND 3 — LIVE, WITH THE ANTI-VACUITY CONTROL BESIDE IT

Three real `prom/prometheus:v3.12.0` containers on the ansible-rendered file, `/api/v1/rules`:

| leg | the seven comparison rules | the three doors |
|---|---|---|
| A series ABSENT | all `inactive`, 0 alerts — **the defect, live** | `pending` 3 / `pending` 1 / `pending` 1 |
| B series PRESENT and healthy | all `inactive` | all `inactive` — acceptance 3 |
| C one of three probes gone | all `inactive` | `PlexBlackboxProbesAbsent` pending on the one that vanished |

`promtool test rules` on the shipped file drives the same three states past `for:` at a simulated
clock, `SUCCESS` rc=0, including the positive control that `PlexWatchdogProbeBroken` and
`PlexUnreachable` are inactive at the same eval — the harness is alive and the emptiness is the
semantics.

### THE THREE FILED ROWS
